,
\mathrm{CA}_{\mathrm{id}}
,

\dot{x}_{\varepsilon}
,
\tilde{\theta}_{t}
.

D.4

Computational Complexity of
PS
\varepsilon
BAI

We provide analysis for the computational complexity of
PS
\varepsilon
BAI
in this subsection.

We consider the number of these operations: arithmetic (addition and multiplication) operations, logic operations and comparison operations and we also regard
\ln(\cdot)
,
\sqrt{\cdot}
and sampling from a distribution as one step of operation.

We decompose the main loop of Algorithm
1
as follows, where the lines with
O(1)
operations are omitted:

•

Exploration phase (Lines 8 to 11):
O(1)
.

•

Change Detection phase (Lines 12 to 16):

–

The
LCD
subroutine in Line 16 needs
O(wd^{2}+Kd)
operations.

•

Context Alignment phase (Lines 17 to 21):

–

The
LCA
subroutine in Line 19 needs
O((wd^{2}+Kd)N)
operations, as the
LCD
subroutine will be invoked
N
times in the worst case in Line 4 of
LCA
.

–

The reversion procedure in Line 21 needs
O(\gamma wd)
operations.

•

The updating procedure and the stopping rule checking (Lines 25 to 32):

–

Updating
\hat{\theta}_{t,j}
needs
O(d^{2})
operations, as we need to incorporate the latest sample into the estimate.

–

Updating
\hat{p}_{t,j},j\in[N]
requires
N
operations.

–

Updating the confidence radius and the empirical best arm need
O(KN)
operations.

–

The stopping condition in Equation (3.4) requires
O(K)
operations.

We remark that some intermediate results can be stored to avoid repeated computations and we believe the algorithm is efficient overall. In particular, since the reward structure is linear, there are
K
arms, and
N
contexts,
O(d^{2})
,
O(K)
, and
O(N)
operations probably cannot be be avoided.

From the above analysis, in the decreasing order of the number of operations:

•

The Change Detection phase requires the most budget as it will be invoked every
\gamma
time steps.

•

The Context Alignment phase requires the second many operations. While it requires many operations when it is implemented, it will not be called during a stationary segment.

•

The updating procedure uses small portion of the operations, as
w
is usually much greater than
K
and
N
.

•

The exploration phase demands constant operations in each loop.

Appendix E

Auxiliary results

Lemma E.1
.

Let
X_{s}
be the arm drawn with the G-optimal allocation
\lambda^{*}
at time step
s
and
Y_{s,x_{s}}=x_{s}^{\top}\theta_{j_{s}}^{*}+\eta_{s}
be the corresponding return with
\mathbb{E}\eta_{s}=0
,
|x_{s}^{\top}\theta_{j_{s}}^{*}|\leq 1
and
|\eta_{s}|\leq 1
.
Then we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\frac{1}{n}\left|\sum_{s=1}^{n}x^{\top}A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}x_{s}Y_{s,x_{s}}-x^{\top}\theta_{j_{s}}^{*}\right|\geq\epsilon\right]\leq\delta

(E.1)

for all

\epsilon\geq\frac{d}{n}\ln\frac{2}{\delta}+\sqrt{\left(\frac{d}{n}\ln\frac{2}{\delta}\right)^{2}+\frac{4d}{n}\ln\frac{2}{\delta}}
.
In addition, if
n\geq\frac{d}{4}\ln\frac{2}{\delta}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\frac{1}{n}\left|\sum_{s=1}^{n}x^{\top}A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}x_{s}Y_{s,x_{s}}-x^{\top}\theta_{j_{s}}^{*}\right|\geq 5\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}\ln\frac{2}{\delta}}~\right]\leq\delta

(E.2)

Proof.

Note that the arms are selected according to the G-optimal design, and thus
\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\lambda^{*}}[xx^{\top}]=A(\lambda^{*})
. Therefore, for all
s\in[n]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[x^{\top}A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}x_{s}Y_{s,x_{s}}-x^{\top}\theta_{j_{s}}^{*}\right]=0

(E.3)

and

\displaystyle\big|x^{\top}A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}x_{s}Y_{s,x_{s}}-x^{\top}\theta_{j_{s}}^{*}\big|

(E.4)

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle\big|x^{\top}A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}x_{s}x_{s}^{\top}\theta_{j_{s}}^{*}\big|+\big|x^{\top}A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}x_{s}\eta_{s}\big|+1

(E.5)

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle\big|x^{\top}A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}x_{s}\big|\big|x_{s}^{\top}\theta_{j_{s}}^{*}\big|+\big|x^{\top}A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}x_{s}\big|\big|\eta_{s}\big|+1

(E.6)

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle 2\big|x^{\top}A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}x_{s}\big|+1

(E.7)

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle 2\|x\|_{A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}}\|x_{s}\|_{A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}}+1

(E.8)

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle 3d

(E.9)

where we make use of
the property of the G-optimal allocation in the last inequality

\|x\|_{A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}}^{2}\leq d,\;\forall x\in\mathcal{X}.

(E.10)

Additionally,

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\left[\left(x^{\top}A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}x_{s}Y_{s,x_{s}}\right)^{2}\right]

(E.11)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\left[\left(x^{\top}A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}x_{s}(x_{s}^{\top}\theta_{j_{s}}^{*}+\eta_{s})\right)^{2}\right]

(E.12)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\left[\left(x^{\top}A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}x_{s}x_{s}^{\top}\theta_{j_{s}}^{*}\right)^{2}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\left(x^{\top}A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}x_{s}\eta_{s}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+2\mathbb{E}\left[x^{\top}A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}x_{s}x_{s}^{\top}\theta_{j_{s}}^{*}\cdot x^{\top}A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}x_{s}\eta_{s}\right]

(E.13)

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{=}

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\left[\left(x^{\top}A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}x_{s}\right)^{2}\left(x_{s}^{\top}\theta_{j_{s}}^{*}\right)^{2}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\eta_{s}^{2}\left(x^{\top}A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}x_{s}\right)^{2}\right]

(E.14)

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\leq}

\displaystyle\;2\mathbb{E}\left[\left(x^{\top}A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}x_{s}\right)^{2}\right]

(E.15)

\displaystyle\overset{(c)}{=}

\displaystyle\;2\,x^{\top}A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}x

(E.16)

\displaystyle\overset{(d)}{\leq}

\displaystyle\;2d

(E.17)

where we make use of the fact that
\eta_{t}
is zero-mean and is independent of other random variables in
(a)
;

|x_{s}^{\top}\theta_{j_{s}}^{*}|\leq 1
and
\mathbb{P}\left[\eta_{s}^{2}\leq 1\right]=1
in
(b)
;

x_{s}\sim\lambda^{*}
in
(c)
;
and the property of the G-optimal allocation (
E.10
) in
(d)
.

According to the Bernstein’s inequality with (
E.3
), (
E.4
) and (
E.11
),

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{P}\left[\frac{1}{n}\Big|\sum_{s=1}^{n}x^{\top}A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}x_{s}Y_{s,x_{s}}-x^{\top}\theta_{j_{s}}^{*}\Big|\geq\epsilon\right]

(E.18)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;2\exp\left(-\frac{\frac{1}{2}(n\epsilon)^{2}}{n\cdot 2d+dn\epsilon}\right)

(E.19)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;2\exp\left(-\frac{n\epsilon^{2}}{4d+2d\epsilon}\right).

(E.20)

In order to upper bound the error probability by
\delta
, we need

\displaystyle 2\exp\left(-\frac{n\epsilon^{2}}{4d+2d\epsilon}\right)\leq\delta

(E.21)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\quad

\displaystyle\epsilon\geq\frac{d}{n}\ln\frac{2}{\delta}+\sqrt{\left(\frac{d}{n}\ln\frac{2}{\delta}\right)^{2}+\frac{4d}{n}\ln\frac{2}{\delta}}.

(E.22)

In addition, if
n\geq\frac{d}{4}\ln\frac{2}{\delta}
, we have

\displaystyle 5\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}\ln\frac{2}{\delta}}=\frac{5}{2}\max\left\{\frac{d}{n}\ln\frac{2}{\delta},\sqrt{\frac{4d}{n}\ln\frac{2}{\delta}}\right\}\geq\frac{d}{n}\ln\frac{2}{\delta}+\sqrt{\left(\frac{d}{n}\ln\frac{2}{\delta}\right)^{2}+\frac{4d}{n}\ln\frac{2}{\delta}}.

(E.23)

This finishes the proof.
∎

Appendix F

Analysis of
N
\varepsilon
BAI

To analyze the theoretical performance of
N
\varepsilon
BAI
, we first show that it can identify an
\varepsilon
-optimal arm with probability
1-\delta
and derive a high-probability upper bound on its stopping time in Lemma
F.1
.

Lemma F.1

(High-probability upper bound of
N
\varepsilon
BAI
)
.

With probability
1-\delta
, the N
\varepsilon
BAI algorithm identifies an
\varepsilon
-optimal arm after at most

\displaystyle\tilde{O}\left(\frac{L_{\max}+d}{\left(\varepsilon+\Delta_{\min}\right)^{2}}\ln\frac{1}{\delta}\right)

time steps, where

\Delta_{\min}=\min\limits_{i\neq i^{*}}\Delta({x^{*}}-x_{i})
.

Next, we prove that after a sufficiently large number of time steps, the probability that
N
\varepsilon
BAI
does not terminate is small in Lemma
F.2
.

Lemma F.2
.

Let

\displaystyle T_{0}=\frac{768(8L_{\max}+25d)}{\left(\varepsilon+\Delta_{\min}\right)^{2}}\ln\frac{768KC_{3}(8L_{\max}+25d)}{\left(\varepsilon+\Delta_{\min}\right)^{2}\delta}

with
C_{3}=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}n^{-3}
.
For
t\geq T_{0}
, the probability that
N
\varepsilon
BAI
does not terminate before
t
time steps is
\frac{\delta}{(\alpha-1)C_{3}(t/2)^{2}}
.

Lastly, we apply Lemmas
F.1
and
F.2
to prove Proposition
3.1
.

See
3.1

The detailed analysis is presented as below.

F.1

Detailed analysis of
N
\varepsilon
BAI

Proof of Lemma
F.1
.

The result is to be proven with the following procedures.

First step
: Prove
\left|x^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{\theta\sim P_{\theta}}\theta-x^{\top}\tilde{\theta}_{t}\right|
can be bounded with high probability.

Let
\bar{\theta}_{t}=\frac{1}{t}\sum_{s=1}^{t}\theta_{j_{s}}^{*}
.
(i) For all
\varepsilon>0
, we have,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[~\left|x^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{\theta\sim P_{\theta}}\theta_{t}-x^{\top}\bar{\theta}\right|>\varepsilon\right]=\mathbb{P}\left[~\left|tx^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{\theta\sim P_{\theta}}\theta-x^{\top}\left(\sum_{l=1}^{N_{t}}L_{l}\theta^{*}{j_{c_{l}}}\right)\right|>t\varepsilon\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left[~\left|\sum_{l=1}^{N_{t}}L_{l}\left(x^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{\theta\sim P_{\theta}}\theta-L_{l}x^{\top}\theta^{*}{j_{c_{l}}}\right)\right|>t\varepsilon\right]\overset{(a)}{\leq}2\exp\left(-\frac{t^{2}\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sum_{l=1}^{N_{t}}(2L_{l})^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\leq}2\exp\left(-\frac{t\varepsilon^{2}}{8L_{\max}}\right).

Since
|x^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{\theta\sim P_{\theta}}\theta|\leq 1
and
x^{\top}\theta^{*}{j_{c_{l}}}|\leq 1
for all
l
,
we obtain (a) by applying Hoeffding’s inequality. We obtain (b) from the fact that
\sum_{l=1}^{N_{t}}L_{l}=t
and
L_{l}\leq L_{\max}
for all
l
.
In other words, for all
\delta>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[~\left|x^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{\theta\sim P_{\theta}}\theta-x^{\top}\bar{\theta}\right|>\sqrt{\frac{8L_{\max}}{t}\ln\frac{2}{\delta}}~\right]\leq\delta.

(ii)
Besides, according to Lemma
E.1
, if
t\geq\frac{d}{4}\ln\frac{2}{\delta}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\left|x^{\top}\tilde{\theta}_{t}-x^{\top}\bar{\theta}_{t}\right|\geq 5\sqrt{\frac{d}{t}\ln\frac{2}{\delta}}~\right]=\mathbb{P}\left[\frac{1}{t}\left|\sum_{s=1}^{t}x^{\top}A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}x_{s}Y_{s,x_{s}}-x^{\top}\theta_{j_{s}}^{*}\right|\geq 5\sqrt{\frac{d}{t}\ln\frac{2}{\delta}}~\right]\leq\delta.

(F.1)

(iii) For all
t>0
, all
x\in\mathcal{X}
, since

\displaystyle\left|x^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{\theta\sim P_{\theta}}\theta-x^{\top}\tilde{\theta}_{t}\right|\leq\left|x^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{\theta\sim P_{\theta}}\theta-x^{\top}\bar{\theta}\right|+\left|x^{\top}\tilde{\theta}_{t}-x^{\top}\bar{\theta}_{t}\right|,

we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\left|x^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{\theta\sim P_{\theta}}\theta-x^{\top}\tilde{\theta}_{t}\right|>\tilde{\rho}_{t}(\alpha)\right]\leq\frac{\delta}{KC_{\alpha}t^{\alpha}}\quad\text{ when }\quad t\geq\frac{d}{4}\ln\frac{4KC_{\alpha}t^{\alpha}}{\delta},

where

\displaystyle\tilde{\rho}_{t}(\alpha)=\sqrt{\frac{8L_{\max}}{t}\ln\frac{4KC_{\alpha}t^{\alpha}}{\delta}}+5\sqrt{\frac{d}{t}\ln\frac{4KC_{\alpha}t^{\alpha}}{\delta}},\quad C_{\alpha}=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}n^{-\alpha},\quad\alpha>2.

(F.2)

For simplicity, we set
\alpha=3
and write
\tilde{\rho}_{t}(3)
as
\tilde{\rho}_{t}
in the following analysis.
We now show that
t\geq\frac{d}{4}\ln\frac{4KC_{\alpha}t^{\alpha}}{\delta}
holds when

t\geq 3d\ln\frac{6dKC_{3}}{\delta}

with the following lemma (the proof of which is presented by the end of Appendix
F.1
).

Lemma F.3
.

Let
a,b,c>0
and
ac\geq 1/2
, then

\displaystyle t\geq\max\{2a\ln b,\,4ac\ln(2ac)\}~\Rightarrow~t\geq a\ln(bt^{c}).

(F.3)

With

\displaystyle a=\frac{d}{4},\quad b=\frac{4KC_{\alpha}}{\delta}=\frac{4KC_{3}}{\delta},\quad c=\alpha=3,

(F.4)

Lemma
F.3
implies that

\displaystyle t\geq 4ac\ln(2abc)=3d\ln\frac{6dKC_{3}}{\delta}~\Rightarrow~t\geq\max\{2a\ln b,\,4ac\ln(2ac)\}~\Rightarrow~t\geq a\ln(bt^{c}).

(F.5)

Define event
\tilde{E}_{t}=\bigcap\limits_{x\in\mathcal{X}}\left\{\left|x^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{\theta\sim P_{\theta}}\theta-x^{\top}\tilde{\theta}_{t}\right|\leq\tilde{\rho}_{t}(\alpha)\right\}
for
t\geq t^{*}

and
\tilde{E}=\bigcap\limits_{t\geq t^{*}}\tilde{E}_{t}
. We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\tilde{E}_{t}^{\mathsf{c}}\right]\leq\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}}\frac{\delta}{KC_{\alpha}t^{\alpha}}=\frac{\delta}{C_{\alpha}t^{\alpha}},\quad\mathbb{P}\left[\tilde{E}^{\mathsf{c}}\right]\leq\sum_{t=t^{*}}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\tilde{E}_{t}^{\mathsf{c}}\right]\leq\sum_{t=1}^{\infty}\frac{\delta}{C_{\alpha}t^{\alpha}}=\delta.

We assume
\tilde{E}
holds in the second and third steps in this proof.

Second step
: Prove
N
\varepsilon
BAI

recommends an
\varepsilon
-optimal arm when it stops.

If the algorithm terminates and returns
\dot{x}_{\varepsilon}\neq{x^{*}}
,
we have

\displaystyle\dot{x}_{\varepsilon}^{\top}\tilde{\theta}_{t}-\tilde{\rho}_{t}+\varepsilon\geq\max_{x\neq\dot{x}_{\varepsilon}}x^{\top}\tilde{\theta}_{t}+\tilde{\rho}_{t}.

Since

\displaystyle\dot{x}_{\varepsilon}^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{\theta\sim P_{\theta}}\theta+\varepsilon\geq\dot{x}_{\varepsilon}^{\top}\tilde{\theta}_{t}-\tilde{\rho}_{t}+\varepsilon\quad\text{and}\quad\max_{x\neq\dot{x}_{\varepsilon}}x^{\top}\tilde{\theta}_{t}+\tilde{\rho}_{t}\geq\max_{x\neq\dot{x}_{\varepsilon}}x^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{\theta\sim P_{\theta}}\theta\geq{x^{*}}^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{\theta\sim P_{\theta}}\theta,

(F.6)

we have

\displaystyle\dot{x}_{\varepsilon}^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{\theta\sim P_{\theta}}\theta+\varepsilon\geq{x^{*}}^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{\theta\sim P_{\theta}}\theta,

(F.7)

implying that
\dot{x}_{\varepsilon}\in\mathcal{X}_{\varepsilon}
. Hence, we always have
\dot{x}_{\varepsilon}\in\mathcal{X}_{\varepsilon}
.

Third step
: Derive the stopping time of
N
\varepsilon
BAI
.

(i) Let
x_{(2)}=\argmax_{x\neq x^{*}}x^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{\theta\sim P_{\theta}}\theta

and
\Delta_{\min}=\min_{x\neq x^{*}}\Delta(x^{*}-x)=(x^{*}-x_{(2)})^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{\theta\sim P_{\theta}}\theta
. We apply the following lemma to show that
N
\varepsilon
BAI
stops when
\tilde{\rho}_{t}
is sufficiently small.

Lemma F.4

(
30
)
.

Denote by
\hat{i}
the index of the item with empirical mean is
i
-th largest: i.e.,
\hat{w}(\hat{1})\geq\ldots\geq\hat{w}(\hat{L}).
Assume that the empirical means of the arms are controlled by
\varepsilon:
i.e.,
\forall i,\left|\hat{w}(i)-w(i)\right|<\varepsilon
. Then,

\forall i,w(i)-\varepsilon\leq\hat{w}(\hat{i})\leq w(i)+\varepsilon.

(F.8)

Lemma
F.4
implies that

\displaystyle\dot{x}_{t}^{\top}\tilde{\theta}_{t}-\tilde{\rho}_{t}+\varepsilon\geq{x^{*}}^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{\theta\sim P_{\theta}}\theta-2\tilde{\rho}_{t}+\varepsilon=x_{(2)}^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{\theta\sim P_{\theta}}\theta-2\tilde{\rho}_{t}+\varepsilon+\Delta_{\min}\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\max_{x\neq\dot{x}_{t}}x^{\top}\tilde{\theta}_{t}+\tilde{\rho}_{t}\leq x_{(2)}^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{\theta\sim P_{\theta}}\theta+2\tilde{\rho}_{t}.

When
\tilde{\rho}_{t}\leq(\varepsilon+\Delta_{\min})/4
, we have

\displaystyle\dot{x}_{t}^{\top}\tilde{\theta}_{t}-\tilde{\rho}_{t}+\varepsilon\geq x_{(2)}^{\top}\tilde{\theta}_{t}+\frac{\varepsilon+\Delta_{\min}}{2}\geq x_{(2)}^{\top}\tilde{\theta}_{t}+2\tilde{\rho}_{t}\geq\max_{x\neq\dot{x}_{t}}x^{\top}\tilde{\theta}_{t}+\tilde{\rho}_{t},

which will lead to the termination of
N
\varepsilon
BAI
.

(ii)
According to the definition of
\tilde{\rho}_{t}
, we have

\displaystyle\tilde{\rho}_{t}(\alpha)\leq(\varepsilon+\Delta_{\min})/4

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow~

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{8L_{\max}}{t}\ln\frac{4KC_{\alpha}t^{\alpha}}{\delta}}+5\sqrt{\frac{d}{t}\ln\frac{4KC_{\alpha}t^{\alpha}}{\delta}}\leq(\varepsilon+\Delta_{\min})/4

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow~

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{1}{t}\ln\frac{4KC_{\alpha}t^{\alpha}}{\delta}}\left(\sqrt{8L_{\max}}+5\sqrt{d}\right)\leq(\varepsilon+\Delta_{\min})/4

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow~

\displaystyle t\geq\frac{\left(\sqrt{8L_{\max}}+5\sqrt{d}\right)^{2}}{\left(\varepsilon+\Delta_{\min}\right)^{2}/16}\ln\frac{4KC_{\alpha}t^{\alpha}}{\delta}

\displaystyle\Leftarrow~

\displaystyle t\geq\frac{32(8L_{\max}+25d)}{\left(\varepsilon+\Delta_{\min}\right)^{2}}\ln\frac{4KC_{\alpha}t^{\alpha}}{\delta}

\displaystyle\Leftarrow~

\displaystyle t\geq\frac{192(8L_{\max}+25d)}{\left(\varepsilon+\Delta_{\min}\right)^{2}}\ln\frac{768KC_{3}(8L_{\max}+25d)}{\left(\varepsilon+\Delta_{\min}\right)^{2}\delta}.

We apply Lemma
F.3
to invert the last line above.
With

\displaystyle a=\frac{32(8L_{\max}+25d)}{\left(\varepsilon+\Delta_{\min}\right)^{2}},\quad b=\frac{4KC_{\alpha}}{\delta},\quad c=\alpha=3,

(F.9)

Lemma
F.3
indicates that

\displaystyle t\geq 4ac\ln(2abc)=\frac{384(8L_{\max}+25d)}{\left(\varepsilon+\Delta_{\min}\right)^{2}}\ln\frac{768KC_{3}(8L_{\max}+25d)}{\left(\varepsilon+\Delta_{\min}\right)^{2}\delta}

(F.10)

\displaystyle~\Rightarrow~

\displaystyle t\geq\max\{2a\ln b,\,4ac\ln(2ac)\}~\Rightarrow~t\geq a\ln(bt^{c}).

(F.11)

Altogether
, we show that with probability
1-\delta
,
N
\varepsilon
BAI
identifies an
\varepsilon
-optimal arm after at most

\displaystyle\frac{384(8L_{\max}+25d)}{\left(\varepsilon+\Delta_{\min}\right)^{2}}\ln\frac{768KC_{3}(8L_{\max}+25d)}{\left(\varepsilon+\Delta_{\min}\right)^{2}\delta}

time steps.

∎

Proof of Lemma
F.2
.

To begin with, let

\displaystyle T_{0}=\frac{768(8L_{\max}+25d)}{\left(\varepsilon+\Delta_{\min}\right)^{2}}\ln\frac{768KC_{3}(8L_{\max}+25d)}{\left(\varepsilon+\Delta_{\min}\right)^{2}\delta}

For any
T\geq T_{0}
, let
\bar{T}=\lceil T/2\rceil
.

(I)

If
N
\varepsilon
BAI
terminates within
\bar{T}
time steps, there is nothing to prove.

(II)

Assume
N
\varepsilon
BAI
does not terminates within
\bar{T}
time steps.
According to the analysis in the proof of Lemma
F.1
, if
N
\varepsilon
BAI
does not terminate before
T
time steps, i.e., the stopping time
\tau
satisfies that
\tau\geq\bar{T}
, then event
\bigcap\limits_{t=\bar{T}+1}^{T-1}\tilde{E}_{t}
does not hold. This indicates

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\tau\geq T|\tau\geq\bar{T}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left[\bigcup_{t=\bar{T}+1}^{T-1}\tilde{E}_{t}^{\mathsf{c}}\right]\leq\sum_{t=\bar{T}}^{T-1}\mathbb{P}\left[\tilde{E}_{t}^{\mathsf{c}}\right]\leq\sum_{t=\bar{T}}^{T-1}\frac{\delta}{C_{\alpha}t^{\alpha}}\leq\frac{\delta}{C_{\alpha}}\int_{\bar{T}}^{T}t^{-\alpha}~\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\delta}{(\alpha-1)C_{\alpha}}\left(\frac{1}{(\bar{T})^{\alpha-1}}-\frac{1}{T^{\alpha}}\right)\overset{(a)}{\leq}\frac{\delta}{(\alpha-1)C_{\alpha}(T/2)^{\alpha-1}}.

(a) results from the fact that
\bar{T}\geq T/2
.

Altogether
, for
T\geq T_{0}
, the probability
N
\varepsilon
BAI
does not terminate before
T
time steps is

\frac{\delta}{(\alpha-1)C_{\alpha}(T/2)^{\alpha-1}}
.

∎

Proof of Proposition
3.1
.

First, Tonelli’s Theorem implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tau]=\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{\tau}1~\mathrm{d}x\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{+\infty}\mathbb{I}(\tau>x)~\mathrm{d}x\right]=\int_{0}^{+\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{I}(\tau>x)\right]~\mathrm{d}x=\int_{0}^{+\infty}\mathbb{P}(\tau>x)~\mathrm{d}x.

Next, we apply Lemma
F.2
to show

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\tau

\displaystyle\leq T_{0}+\mathbb{E}[\tau|\tau\geq T_{0}+1]\cdot\mathbb{P}[\tau\geq T_{0}+1]\leq T_{0}+\int_{T_{0}}^{+\infty}\mathbb{P}(\tau\geq x)~\mathrm{d}x

(F.12)

\displaystyle\leq T_{0}+\int_{T_{0}}^{+\infty}\frac{\delta}{(\alpha-1)C_{\alpha}(x/2)^{\alpha-1}}~\mathrm{d}x=T_{0}+\frac{\delta}{(\alpha-1)(\alpha-2)(T_{0}/2)^{\alpha-2}}.

(F.13)

Besides, Lemma
F.1
indicates that
N
\varepsilon
BAI
can identify an
\varepsilon
-optimal arm with probability
1-\delta
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
F.3
.

For
x>0
, let

\displaystyle f(x)=x-a\ln(bx^{c})=x-a\ln b-ac\ln x=x\left(1-\frac{a\ln b}{x}-\frac{ac\ln x}{x}\right).

Since

\displaystyle f(x)\geq 0~\Leftarrow~\left\{\begin{array}[]{l }\displaystyle\vphantom{\Bigg(}\frac{a\ln b}{x}\leq\frac{1}{2}\\
\displaystyle\frac{ac\ln x}{x}\leq\frac{1}{2}\end{array}\right.~\Leftrightarrow~\left\{\begin{array}[]{l }\displaystyle\vphantom{\bigg(}x\geq 2a\ln b\\
\displaystyle x\geq 2ac\ln x\end{array}\right..

Let
d=2ac
,
x=d\ln(dy)
, then

\displaystyle x\geq 2ac\ln x=d\ln x~\Leftrightarrow~d\ln(dy)\geq d\ln(d\ln(dy))~\Leftrightarrow~\ln(dy)\geq\ln(d\ln(dy))~\Leftrightarrow~y\geq\ln(dy).

Since
z^{0.4}\geq\ln z
for all
z\geq 1
, we have
\ln(dy)\leq(dy)^{0.4}\leq\sqrt{dy}
and

\displaystyle y\geq\ln(dy)~\Leftarrow~y\geq\sqrt{dy}~\Leftarrow~y\geq d

(F.18)

when
yd\geq 1
.
Hence, when
ac\geq 1/2
,
y\geq d=2ac\geq 1
, we have
y\geq\ln(dy)
.
Furthermore, for
x
such that
x\geq\max\{2a\ln b,\,4ac\ln(2ac)\}
, we have
f(x)\geq 0
.

∎

Appendix G

Proof Outline of Theorem
3.2

A proof outline of Theorem
3.2
is provided in this section. It consists of three steps:

Step
1
:

PS
\varepsilon
BAI
(Algorithm
1
) depends on the success of change detection and context alignment (Algorithm
3
and
4
). We firstly upper bound the failure probability of these two subroutines via Lemma
G.1
, Lemma
G.2
, Lemma
G.3
and summarized in Lemma
G.4
. More details about these two subroutines are provided in Appendix
D.2.2
and the proof of the Lemmas are postponed to Appendix
H
.

Step
2
:
Subsequently, conditioned on their success, we provide a theoretical guarantee for the choice of the confidence radius
\rho_{t}(x,\tilde{x})
for
\Delta(x,\tilde{x})
at time step
t
in Lemma
G.5
. The proof is detailed in Appendix
I
.

Step
3
:
Lastly, utilizing the above elements, we provide a sufficient condition for the stopping rule, and upper bound the number of time steps in Exp phases
T_{\tau}
via Lemma
G.6
whose proof is presented in Appendix
J
.
As the total number of time steps
\tau
is upper bounded by a constant multiple of
T_{\tau}
, the high-probability upper bound on the stopping time
\tau
is obtained. This finishes the proof of Theorem
3.2
(please refer to Appendix
J
).

Step
1
:

We borrow the terminology in hypothesis testing to define the errors. Let the null hypothesis be:
the algorithm has undergone a changepoint within the last
w
CD samples
.
For Algorithm
3
, we will characterize the type I error:
the algorithm has experienced a changepoint within the last
w
CD samples but it fails to raise a changing alarm
, and the type II error:
a changepoint has not occurred but Algorithm
3
raises a changing alarm
.
We refer to the event Failed Alarm (
FA
) and the event False Alarm Error (
FAE
) as that a type I error occurs and that a type II error occurs, respectively:

\displaystyle{\rm FA}_{l}:=\{\mbox{a type I error occurs at changepoint }c_{l}\},l\in\mathbb{N},

(G.1)

\displaystyle{\rm FAE}_{t}:=\{\mbox{a type II error occurs time step }t\},t\in\mathcal{T}_{{\rm FAE}},

(G.2)

\displaystyle{\rm FA}:=\bigcup_{\{l:c_{l}\leq\tau\}}{\rm FA}_{l}\quad\mbox{ and }\quad{\rm FAE}:=\bigcup_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{{\rm FAE}}}{\rm FAE}_{t},

(G.3)

where
\mathcal{T}_{{\rm FAE}}:=\{t:t\mbox{ in CD phase},t\leq\tau,[t-w\gamma,t]\cap\mathcal{C}=\emptyset\}
and
\tau
is the stopping time. In term of Algorithm
4
, define the event
{\rm MI}_{l}:=\{\mbox{Algorithm~\ref{alg:CA} misidentifies }\theta_{j_{t}}^{*}\},\;l\in\mathbb{N}
and
{\rm MI}:=\bigcup_{\{l:c_{l}\leq\tau\}}{\rm MI}_{l}
. Define the good event

\displaystyle{\rm Good}:=\{{\rm FA}^{c}\cap{\rm FAE}^{c}\cap{\rm MI}^{c}\}.

(G.4)

We upper bound the failure probability of
Good
in Lemma
G.1
, Lemma
G.2
, Lemma
G.3
and conclude the results in Lemma
G.4
.

Lemma G.1
.

For any
\delta_{{\rm FAE}}\in(0,1)
,
with
b\geq\frac{8d}{3w}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{{\rm FAE}}}+\sqrt{\left(\frac{8d}{3w}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{{\rm FAE}}}\right)^{2}+\frac{24}{w}d\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{{\rm FAE}}}}
,

LCD
makes no false alarm before the stopping time
\tau
with probability at least

\mathbb{P}[{\rm FAE}^{c}]\geq 1-\frac{\tau}{\gamma}K\delta_{{\rm FAE}}.

(G.5)

Lemma
G.1
can also be stated as,
fix
b
,

\mathbb{P}\left[{\rm FAE}^{c}\right]\geq 1-\frac{\tau}{\gamma}2K\exp\left(-\min\left\{\frac{3wb}{20d},\frac{wb^{2}}{150d}\right\}\right),

(G.6)

which indicates we can always decrease the error probability by enlarging the window size
w
.

Assume
c_{l}
is observable, i.e.,
\theta_{j_{c_{l}}}^{*}\neq\theta_{j_{c_{l+1}}}^{*}
, and denote
\hat{c}_{l}
as the alarm time of
c_{l}
from Algorithm
3
.

Lemma G.2
.

Conditional on
{\rm FAE}^{c}
, for any
\delta_{{\rm FA}}\in(0,1)
, if
\frac{\Delta_{c}-b}{2}\geq\frac{d}{w}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{{\rm FA}}}+\sqrt{\left(\frac{d}{w}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{{\rm FA}}}\right)^{2}+\frac{4d}{w}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{{\rm FA}}}}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[c_{l}\leq\hat{c}_{l}\leq c_{l}+\frac{w\gamma}{2}\big|\hat{c}_{l}\geq c_{l}\right]\geq 1-\delta_{{\rm FA}}.

(G.7)

In addition,
\mathbb{P}\left[{\rm FA}\big|{\rm FAE}^{c}\right]\leq l_{\tau}\delta_{{\rm FA}}
.

Lemma
G.2
guarantees a prompt change alarm will be raised within
\frac{w\gamma}{2}
time steps after a changepoint occurs. This also explains why
\frac{w\gamma}{2}
samples are abandoned at line
16
of Algorithm
1
so that
\mathcal{T}_{t,j}
only keeps samples from context
j
.

Lemma G.3
.

Conditional on
{\rm FAE}^{c}
and
{\rm FA}^{c}
, based on the conditions in Lemma
G.1
and Lemma
G.2
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[{\rm MI}|{\rm FAE}^{c},{\rm FA}^{c}\right]\leq l_{\tau}\left[(N-1)\delta_{{\rm FA}}+K\delta_{{\rm FAE}}\right].

(G.8)

The intuition of Lemma
G.3
is simple: change alarm should not be raised when the samples are from the same context and, change alarms should be raised when the samples are from the other
(N-1)
contexts.
The error made by Algorithm
3
and
4
can be concluded as follows

Lemma G.4
.

Assume the instance satisfies Assumption
1
,

\mathbb{P}\left[{\rm Good}\right]\geq 1-\frac{\delta}{2\tau^{*}}\geq 1-\frac{\delta}{2}.

(G.9)

Step
2
:

To give an upper bound on
T_{\tau}
, we firstly prove that the estimate of
\Delta(x,\tilde{x})
at time

is within distance
\rho_{t}(x,\tilde{x})
from the ground truth w.h.p.
Define the event

as the estimates of all the mean gaps locate in the high-probability
C
onfidence
I
nterval

{\rm CI}_{t}:=\{\big|\hat{\Delta}_{t}(x,\tilde{x})-\Delta(x,\tilde{x})\big|\leq\rho_{t}(x,\tilde{x}),\forall x,\tilde{x}\in\mathcal{X}\}

(G.10)

and

where

).

Lemma G.5
.

If
T_{t}\geq\frac{2L_{\max}}{9}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[{\rm CI}_{t}\big|{\rm Good}\right]\geq 1-\left(K\delta_{v,T_{t}}+N\delta_{d,T_{t}}+KN\delta_{m,T_{t}}\right).

(G.11)

In addition,

\mathbb{P}\left[{\rm CI}\big|{\rm Good}\right]\geq 1-\frac{\delta}{2}.

(G.12)

In addition to an estimation over the latent vector which is sufficient under the stationary case (
\alpha_{t}
in

,

and the ground truth
\mathbf{p}
(

),
as well as the interactions between the latent vectors and the distribution (

in

).
This is reflected by

and

which bound the
the failure probability of Vector Estimation, Distribution Estimation and Residual Estimation, respectively.

:

.

, the recommended arm

).

.

Appendix H

Analysis of
PS
\varepsilon
BAI
: Change Detection and Context Alignment

As the output of Algorithms
3
and
4
depends on random samples,
the output may not meet our expectation with some probabilities.
We will characterize the probabilities of the three “bad” events:
FAE
in Appendix
H.1
,
FA
in Appendix
H.2
and
MI
in Appendix
H.3
; and finally upper bound the failure probability of the good event
{\rm Good}
in this section.
See
G.4

Some notations are introduced first

•

\theta_{1:\infty}:=(\theta_{j_{t}}^{*})_{t=1}^{\infty}
is the latent vector sequence.

•

\hat{c}_{l}
indicates the time step when we raise alarm for the
l^{\rm th}
changepoint.

According to the dynamics (see Dynamics
1
) of the problem, at a changepoint
c_{l}\in\mathcal{C}
, the next latent vector will be sampled from
P_{\theta}
. Therefore, it may happen that
\theta_{j_{c_{l}}}^{*}=\theta_{j_{c_{l}-1}}^{*}
, i.e, this changepoint
c_{l}
is hidden and these two consecutive stationary segments are observed as one stationary segment. In order to upper bound the errors made by Algorithm
3
, we make the following assumption, which yields the worst-case scenario in terms of the error probability.

Assumption 2
.

Given
\theta_{1:\infty}
and the changepoint sequence
\mathcal{C}
,
\theta_{j_{c_{l}}}^{*}\neq\theta_{j_{c_{l+1}}}^{*},\forall l\in\mathbb{N}
.

In this section, we do not consider the randomness of
\theta_{1:\infty}
,
i.e., we consider a realization of the sequence.
4
4

4

This can also be taken as, we are conditioning on
\theta_{1:\infty}
, as the realization of the sequence is independent from the behavior of any agent/algorithm.

The results do not involve any parameter depending on the specific sequence and thus can be applied to any latent vector sequence.

H.1

False Alarm Error

See
G.1

Proof of Lemma
G.1
.

Note that within a stationary segment of length
t
, the observations are i.i.d., and thus the time segments between two consecutive false alarm time
\{\hat{c}_{l}-\hat{c}_{l-1}\}_{l=1}^{t}
(where
\hat{c}_{0}=0
) are i.i.d. i.e., it is a renewal process.
Thus, it is sufficient to bound the error probability under the stationary case, i.e.
\mathcal{C}=\emptyset
and
\theta_{1:\infty}=(\theta_{j_{t}}^{*}=\theta_{j_{1}}^{*})_{t=1}^{\infty}
.

Assume that
we are under a stationary segment of length
t
. We wish to upper bound the error
\mathbb{P}[{\rm FAE}]=\mathbb{P}[\hat{c}_{1}\leq t]
. This is given by Lemma
H.1
.

Lemma H.1
.

Given
w
CD samples from the same context
[(\tilde{x}_{s},Y_{s,\tilde{x}_{s}})]_{s=1}^{w}
in a CD phase at time step
t
, Algorithm
3
makes a false alarm error with probability upper bounded by
K\delta_{{\rm FAE}}
, i.e.

\mathbb{P}\left[{\rm FAE}_{t}\right]\leq K\delta_{{\rm FAE}}

(H.1)

if
b\geq\frac{8d}{3w}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{{\rm FAE}}}+\sqrt{\left(\frac{8d}{3w}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{{\rm FAE}}}\right)^{2}+\frac{24}{w}d\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{{\rm FAE}}}}
.

Assume
\gamma
divides
t
.
In the case of a stationary segment, the input samples to Algorithm
3
are
\left\{[(x_{s\gamma},Y_{s\gamma,x_{s\gamma}})]_{s=k}^{k+w-1}\right\}_{k=1}^{\frac{t}{\gamma}}

if no changing alarm is raised (We ignore the initialization at Line
5
in Algorithm
1
for simplicity).
Denote
\Gamma_{i}(t):=\{k\gamma:k\equiv i\mod w,k\gamma\leq t\},i=0,1,\dots,w-1
.
Given any
i=0,\ldots,w-1
, for any
k_{1}\neq k_{2}\in\Gamma_{i}(t)
, the two list of samples,
[(x_{s\gamma},Y_{s\gamma,x_{s\gamma}})]_{s=k_{1}}^{k_{1}+w-1}
and
[(x_{s\gamma},Y_{s\gamma,x_{s\gamma}})]_{s=k_{2}}^{k_{2}+w-1}
, do not overlap (thus, they are independent). Therefore,

\mathbb{P}[k]:=\mathbb{P}[\hat{c}_{1}=k\gamma+i,\hat{c}_{1}\in\Gamma_{i}(t)]
is a geometric distribution with parameter upper bounded by
K\delta_{FAE}
. We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\hat{c}_{1}\in\Gamma_{i}(t)\right]\leq 1-\left(1-K\delta_{{\rm FAE}}\right)^{|\Gamma_{i}(t)|}.

(H.2)

Hence, the cumulative false alarm error is

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}[{\rm FAE}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left[\hat{c}_{1}\leq t\right]

(H.3)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{w-1}\left[1-\left(1-K\delta_{{\rm FAE}}\right)^{|\Gamma_{i}(t)|}\right]

(H.4)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=0}^{w-1}|\Gamma_{i}(t)|K\delta_{{\rm FAE}}

(H.5)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{t}{\gamma}K\delta_{{\rm FAE}}.

(H.6)

∎

Proof of Lemma
H.1
.

According to Algorithm
3
, given
w
CD samples from the same context
[(\tilde{x}_{s},Y_{s,\tilde{x}_{s}})]_{s=1}^{w}
and
x\in\mathcal{X}
, we need to bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\Big|\sum_{s=1}^{\frac{w}{2}}x^{\top}A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}\tilde{x}_{s}Y_{s,\tilde{x}_{s}}-\sum_{s=\frac{w}{2}+1}^{w}x^{\top}A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}\tilde{x}_{s}Y_{s,\tilde{x}_{s}}\Big|\geq\frac{w}{2}b\right].

(H.7)

The proof is similar to Lemma
E.1
.
For any
s\in[\frac{w}{2}]
and
\tilde{s}=s+\frac{w}{2}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[x^{\top}A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}\tilde{x}_{s}Y_{s,\tilde{x}_{s}}-x^{\top}A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}\tilde{x}_{\tilde{s}}Y_{\tilde{s},\tilde{x}_{\tilde{s}}}\right]=0

(H.8)

and

\displaystyle\big|x^{\top}A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}\tilde{x}_{s}Y_{s,\tilde{x}_{s}}-x^{\top}A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}\tilde{x}_{\tilde{s}}Y_{\tilde{s},\tilde{x}_{\tilde{s}}}\big|

(H.9)

\displaystyle\leq\big|x^{\top}A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}\tilde{x}_{s}\tilde{x}_{s}^{\top}\theta_{j_{s}}^{*}\big|+\big|x^{\top}A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}\tilde{x}_{s}\eta_{s}\big|+\big|x^{\top}A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}\tilde{x}_{\tilde{s}}\tilde{x}_{\tilde{s}}^{\top}\theta_{j_{\tilde{s}}}^{*}\big|+\big|x^{\top}A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}\tilde{x}_{\tilde{s}}\eta_{\tilde{s}}\big|

(H.10)

\displaystyle\leq 4d.

(H.11)

By making use of (
E.11
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(x^{\top}A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}\tilde{x}_{s}Y_{s,\tilde{x}_{s}}-x^{\top}A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}\tilde{x}_{\tilde{s}}Y_{\tilde{s},\tilde{x}_{\tilde{s}}}\right)^{2}\right]

(H.12)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(x^{\top}A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}\tilde{x}_{s}Y_{s,\tilde{x}_{s}}\right)^{2}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\left(x^{\top}A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}\tilde{x}_{\tilde{s}}Y_{\tilde{s},\tilde{x}_{\tilde{s}}}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad-2\mathbb{E}\left[x^{\top}A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}\tilde{x}_{s}Y_{s,\tilde{x}_{s}}\cdot x^{\top}A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}\tilde{x}_{\tilde{s}}Y_{\tilde{s},\tilde{x}_{\tilde{s}}}\right]

(H.13)

\displaystyle\leq 4d+2\mathbb{E}\left[|x^{\top}\theta_{j_{s}}^{*}\cdot x^{\top}\theta_{j_{\tilde{s}}}^{*}|\right]

(H.14)

\displaystyle\leq 6d.

(H.15)

According to the Bernstein’s inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\Big|\sum_{s=1}^{\frac{w}{2}}\left(x^{\top}A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}\tilde{x}_{s}Y_{s,\tilde{x}_{s}}-x^{\top}A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}\tilde{x}_{s+\frac{w}{2}}Y_{s+\frac{w}{2},\tilde{x}_{s+\frac{w}{2}}}\right)\Big|\geq\frac{w}{2}\epsilon\right]

(H.16)

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\left(-\frac{\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{w}{2}\epsilon\right)^{2}}{\frac{w}{2}\cdot 6d+\frac{4d}{3}\frac{w}{2}\epsilon}\right)

(H.17)

\displaystyle=2\exp\left(-\frac{\frac{w}{2}\epsilon^{2}}{12d+\frac{8d}{3}\epsilon}\right).

(H.18)

In order to upper bound the error probability by
\delta_{{\rm FAE}}
, we need

\displaystyle 2\exp\left(-\frac{\frac{w}{2}\epsilon^{2}}{12d+\frac{8d}{3}\epsilon}\right)\leq\delta_{FAE}

(H.19)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\quad

\displaystyle\epsilon\geq\frac{4d}{3\cdot\frac{w}{2}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{{\rm FAE}}}+\sqrt{\left(\frac{4d}{3\cdot\frac{w}{2}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{{\rm FAE}}}\right)^{2}+12\cdot\frac{2}{w}d\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{{\rm FAE}}}}.

(H.20)

By the choice of
b\geq\frac{8d}{3w}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{{\rm FAE}}}+\sqrt{\left(\frac{8d}{3w}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{{\rm FAE}}}\right)^{2}+\frac{24}{w}d\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{{\rm FAE}}}}
,
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\Big|\frac{2}{w}\sum_{s=1}^{\frac{w}{2}}x^{\top}A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}\tilde{x}_{s}Y_{s,\tilde{x}_{s}}-\frac{2}{w}\sum_{s=\frac{w}{2}+1}^{w}x^{\top}A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}\tilde{x}_{s}Y_{s,\tilde{x}_{s}}\Big|\geq b\right]\leq\delta_{{\rm FAE}}.

(H.21)

A union bound over the
K
arms yields the final result.
∎

H.2

Failed Alarm

See
G.2

Proof of Lemma
G.2
.

Conditioned on
{\rm FAE}^{c}
, the detection at the changepoints is independent. Thus we can assume there is only one changepoint
c_{1}
within a certain number of consecutive time steps.

Algorithm
3
is given
w
CD samples which are collected under two different context.
Without loss of generality, we assume the sample selected at time
c_{1}
is among the CD samples (otherwise, we can regard the time step of the first sample from the second context as
c_{1}
).
We wish to bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[c_{1}\leq\hat{c}_{1}\leq c_{1}+\frac{\gamma w}{2}\big|\hat{c}_{1}\geq c_{1}\right]

(H.22)

which is the probability of the event that, after a changepoint occurs, a changing alarm will be raised within
\frac{w\gamma}{2}
time steps (or
\frac{w}{2}
CD samples).
Here,
\hat{c}_{1}\geq c_{1}
can be guaranteed as we are conditioning on that there is no false alarm error (
{\rm FAE}^{c}
).

The event
c_{1}\leq\hat{c}_{1}\leq c_{1}+\frac{\gamma w}{2}
indicates,
at least one of the CD sample list in

\left\{\left[(x_{c_{1}+(s-k)\gamma},Y_{c_{1}+(s-k)\gamma,x_{c_{1}+(s-k)\gamma}})\right]_{s=1}^{w}\right\}_{k=\frac{w}{2}+1}^{w}

will trigger the changing alarm in Algorithm
3
. In particular, in Lemma
H.2
, we consider the failed arm probability when the CD samples are composed by exactly half samples from each contexts,
\left[(x_{c_{1}+(s-1-\frac{w}{2})\gamma},Y_{c_{1}+(s-1-\frac{w}{2})\gamma,x_{c_{1}+(s-1-\frac{w}{2})\gamma}})\right]_{s=1}^{w}
.

Lemma H.2
.

Given
w
CD samples from the two different contexts
[(\tilde{x}_{s},Y_{s,\tilde{x}_{s}})]_{s=1}^{w}
in a CD phase, where
[(\tilde{x}_{s},Y_{s,\tilde{x}_{s}})]_{s=1}^{\frac{w}{2}}
is from latent vector
\theta_{j}^{*}
and
[(\tilde{x}_{s},Y_{s,\tilde{x}_{s}})]_{s=\frac{w}{2}+1}^{w}
is from latent vector
\theta_{\tilde{j}}^{*}
, Algorithm
3
raises a changing alarm with probability lower bounded by

1-\delta_{{\rm FA}}

(H.23)

if
\frac{\Delta_{c}-b}{2}\geq\frac{d}{w}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{{\rm FA}}}+\sqrt{\left(\frac{d}{w}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{{\rm FA}}}\right)^{2}+\frac{4d}{w}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{{\rm FA}}}}

where
\Delta_{c}:=\max_{x\in\mathcal{X}}|x^{\top}(\theta_{j}^{*}-\theta_{\tilde{j}}^{*})|
and it is assumed to be greater than
b
.

By making use of Lemma
H.2
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[c_{1}\leq\hat{c}_{1}\leq c_{1}+\frac{w\gamma}{2}\big|\hat{c}_{1}\geq c_{1}\right]

(H.24)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left[\exists k\in\{\frac{w}{2}+1,\ldots,w\},\right.

(H.25)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\left.\mbox{{\sc LCD}}\left(w,b,[(x_{c_{1}+(s-k)\gamma},Y_{c_{1}+(s-k)\gamma,x_{c_{1}+(s-k)\gamma}})]_{s=1}^{w}\right)=\mbox{True}\big|\hat{c}_{1}\geq c_{1}\right]

(H.26)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\left[\mbox{{\sc LCD}}\left(w,b,[(x_{c_{1}+(s-1-\frac{w}{2})\gamma},Y_{c_{1}+(s-1-\frac{w}{2})\gamma,x_{c_{1}+(s-1-\frac{w}{2})\gamma}})]_{s=1}^{w}\right)=\mbox{True}\big|\hat{c}_{1}\geq c_{1}\right]

(H.27)

\displaystyle\geq 1-\delta_{{\rm FA}}

(H.28)

where
\Delta_{c}=\max_{x\in\mathcal{X}}|x^{\top}(\theta_{j}^{*}-\theta_{\tilde{j}}^{*})|
and
\theta_{j}^{*},\theta_{\tilde{j}}^{*}
are the latent vectors.
Hence,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[{\rm FA}\big|{\rm FAE}^{c}\right]=1-\mathbb{P}\left[{\rm FA}^{c}\big|{\rm FAE}^{c}\right]\leq 1-\left(1-\delta_{{\rm FA}}\right)^{l_{\tau}}\leq l_{\tau}\delta_{{\rm FA}}

(H.29)

∎

Proof of Lemma
H.2
.

According to the design of
LCD
,
there exists an
x\in\mathcal{X}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[~\left|\frac{2}{w}\sum_{s=1}^{\frac{w}{2}}x^{\top}A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}\tilde{x}_{s}Y_{s,\tilde{x}_{s}}-\frac{2}{w}\sum_{s=\frac{w}{2}+1}^{w}x^{\top}A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}\tilde{x}_{s}Y_{s,\tilde{x}_{s}}\right|\geq b~\right]

(H.30)

\displaystyle\geq 1-\mathbb{P}\Bigg[~\left|\sum_{s=1}^{\frac{w}{2}}x^{\top}A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}\tilde{x}_{s}Y_{s,\tilde{x}_{s}}-\sum_{s=\frac{w}{2}+1}^{w}x^{\top}A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}\tilde{x}_{s}Y_{s,\tilde{x}_{s}}-\frac{w}{2}x^{\top}(\theta_{j}^{*}-\theta_{\tilde{j}}^{*})\right|

(H.31)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\geq\left|\frac{w}{2}b-\frac{w}{2}x^{\top}(\theta_{j}^{*}-\theta_{\tilde{j}}^{*})\right|~\Bigg]

(H.32)

\displaystyle=1-\mathbb{P}\Bigg[~\left|\sum_{s=1}^{\frac{w}{2}}\left(x^{\top}A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}\tilde{x}_{s}Y_{s,\tilde{x}_{s}}-x^{\top}\theta_{j}^{*}\right)-\sum_{s=\frac{w}{2}+1}^{w}\left(x^{\top}A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}\tilde{x}_{s}Y_{s,\tilde{x}_{s}}-x^{\top}\theta_{\tilde{j}}^{*}\right)\right|

(H.33)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\geq\left|\frac{w}{2}b-\frac{w}{2}x^{\top}(\theta_{j}^{*}-\theta_{\tilde{j}}^{*})\right|~\Bigg]

(H.34)

\displaystyle\geq 1-\delta_{{\rm FA}}

(H.35)

where the last inequality holds as
\frac{\Delta_{c}-b}{2}\geq\frac{d}{w}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{{\rm FA}}}+\sqrt{\left(\frac{d}{w}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{{\rm FA}}}\right)^{2}+\frac{4d}{w}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{{\rm FA}}}}.

∎

H.3

Context Alignment

See
G.3

Proof of Lemma
G.3
.

The error of the context alignment procedure can be derived from the FAE and FA analyses.
Conditioned on
{\rm FAE}^{c},{\rm FA}^{c}
and that the previous
l-1
contexts are correctly identified, i.e.,
\bigcap_{k=1}^{l-1}{\rm MI}_{k}^{c}
, we have the following statements.

Firstly, according to Lemma
H.1
, the change alarm at Line
4
in Algorithm
4
will not be triggered with probability at least

(1-K\delta_{{\rm FAE}})

if the current context is context
j
. This error has been taken into account in the FAE (see Remark
H.3
).

Secondly, if the current context is not
j
(which will occur at most
N-1
times), a change alarm will be raised with probability at least

1-\delta_{{\rm FA}}

by Lemma
G.2
.

Therefore, the error probability at
c_{l}
is upper bounded by

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[{\rm MI}_{l}|{\rm FAE}^{c},{\rm FA}^{c},\cap_{k=1}^{l-1}{\rm MI}_{k}^{c}\right]\leq(N-1)\delta_{{\rm FA}}+K\delta_{{\rm FAE}}

(H.36)

and by a union bound, the cumulative error probability bounded by

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[{\rm MI}|{\rm FAE}^{c},{\rm FA}^{c}\right]

\displaystyle=1-\mathbb{P}\left[{\rm MI}^{c}|{\rm FAE}^{c},{\rm FA}^{c}\right]

(H.37)

\displaystyle\leq 1-\left(1-(N-1)\delta_{{\rm FA}}-K\delta_{{\rm FAE}}\right)^{l_{\tau}}

(H.38)

\displaystyle\leq l_{\tau}\left((N-1)\delta_{{\rm FA}}+K\delta_{{\rm FAE}}\right).

(H.39)

∎

Remark H.3
.

Note that (1) we will only bound the
FAE
when the CD samples are from the same context; (2) in the analysis of
FAE
, we bound the error probability on the whole horizon up to time
t
as we assume there is no changepoint. In particular, there will be unused and redundant
\frac{w}{2}K\delta_{{\rm FAE}}
errors budget for
FAE
at each changepoint, which accumulates to
l_{\tau}\frac{w}{2}K\delta_{{\rm FAE}}
before time step
\tau
.
Therefore, the second error term in (
H.37
),
l_{\tau}K\delta_{{\rm FAE}}
, can be covered by the unused
l_{\tau}\frac{w}{2}K\delta_{{\rm FAE}}
error budget from
FAE
, and thus it can be neglected in (
H.37
).

Proof of Lemma
G.4
.

According to Lemma
G.1
,
G.2
,
G.3
, Assumption
1
and Remark
H.3
, given a time
\tau
, the total failure probability is upper bounded by

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[{\rm Good}^{c}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left[{\rm FAE}\cup{\rm FA}\cup{\rm MI}\right]

(H.40)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\tau}{\gamma}K\delta_{{\rm FAE}}+l_{\tau}\delta_{{\rm FA}}+l_{\tau}\cdot(N-1)\delta_{{\rm FA}}

(H.41)

\displaystyle=\frac{\tau}{\gamma}K\delta_{{\rm FAE}}+l_{\tau}N\delta_{{\rm FA}}.

(H.42)

In particular, when
\tau
is upper bounded by
\tau^{*}
in Line
2
of Algorithm
1

\tau\leq\tau^{*}=c_{0}\frac{NL_{\max}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\ln\frac{N^{2}KL_{\max}/\varepsilon^{2}}{\delta}

(H.43)

where
c_{0}=3\cdot 6400\ln 6400
.
The number of changepoints till
\tau
is upper bounded by
l_{\tau^{*}}\leq\frac{\tau^{*}}{L_{\min}}
.

By Assumption
1
, when

b=\frac{8d}{3w}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{{\rm FAE}}}+\sqrt{\left(\frac{8d}{3w}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{{\rm FAE}}}\right)^{2}+\frac{24}{w}d\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{{\rm FAE}}}}

and
\delta_{{\rm FAE}}=\frac{\gamma\delta}{4(\tau^{*})^{2}K}
, the conditions of Lemma
G.1
are met. And we can upper bound
\frac{\tau}{\gamma}K\delta_{{\rm FAE}}
by
\frac{\delta}{4\tau^{*}}\leq\frac{\delta}{4}
.

By making use of Assumption
1
and setting
\delta_{{\rm FA}}=\frac{\delta}{4Nl_{\tau^{*}}\tau^{*}}
,
we have
\delta_{{\rm FA}}>\delta_{{\rm FAE}}
and

\displaystyle\frac{\Delta_{c}-b}{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{b}{2}\geq\frac{4d}{3w}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{{\rm FAE}}}+\sqrt{\left(\frac{4d}{3w}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{{\rm FAE}}}\right)^{2}+\frac{6}{w}d\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{{\rm FAE}}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\frac{d}{w}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{{\rm FA}}}+\sqrt{\left(\frac{d}{w}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{{\rm FA}}}\right)^{2}+\frac{4d}{w}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{{\rm FA}}}}.

(H.44)

Thus, Lemma
G.2
can be applied.
l_{\tau^{*}}N\delta_{{\rm FA}}
can be upper bounded by
\frac{\delta}{4\tau^{*}}\leq\frac{\delta}{4}
.

Therefore, according to (
H.40
),
\mathbb{P}[{\rm Good}]\geq 1-\frac{\delta}{2\tau^{*}}\geq 1-\frac{\delta}{2}
.

∎

Appendix I

Analysis of
PS
\varepsilon
BAI
: Estimation Error

Lemma
G.5
is proved in this section. We will upper bound these three error terms (see (
I.1
)):
VE
error in App
I.1
,
DE
error in App
I.2
,
RE
error in App
I.3
, and finally
we will prove Lemma
G.5
which upper bounds the failure probability of
{\rm CI}
at the end of this section.
See
G.5

Given any two arms
x,\tilde{x}\in\mathcal{X}
,
by the triangular inequality, the deviation between
\hat{\Delta}_{t}(x,\tilde{x})
and
\Delta(x,\tilde{x})
can be upper bounded by three terms: the Vector-Estimation Error (VE) term, the Distribution-Estimation Error (DE) term, and the Residual Estimation Error (RE) term:

\displaystyle|\hat{\Delta}_{t}(x,\tilde{x})-\Delta(x,\tilde{x})|

(I.1)

\displaystyle=|(x-\tilde{x})^{\top}\hat{\Theta}_{t}\hat{\mathbf{p}}_{t}-(x-\tilde{x})^{\top}\Theta\mathbf{p}|

(I.2)

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\Big|\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left(\hat{\Delta}_{t,j}(x,\tilde{x})-\Delta_{j}(x,\tilde{x})\right)\hat{p}_{t,j}\Big|}_{\mbox{VE term}}+\underbrace{\Big|\sum_{j=1}^{N}\hat{\Delta}_{t,j}^{{\rm clip}_{2}}(x,\tilde{x})(\hat{p}_{t,j}-p_{j})\Big|}_{\mbox{DE term}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\underbrace{\Big|\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left(\Delta_{j}(x,\tilde{x})-\hat{\Delta}_{t,j}^{{\rm clip}_{2}}(x,\tilde{x})\right)(\hat{p}_{t,j}-p_{j})\Big|}_{\mbox{RE term}},

(I.3)

where
a^{{\rm clip}_{2}}:={\rm clip}_{2}(a):=\min\{\max\{a,-2\},2\}
is a shorthand notation for the value of
a
that is clipped to the interval
[-2,2]
. The reason the value
\hat{\Delta}_{t,j}(x,\tilde{x})
is clipped is the ground truth
|\Delta_{t,j}(x,\tilde{x})|\leq 2
.

Recall the event
{\rm CI}

\displaystyle{\rm CI}_{t}:=\{\big|\hat{\Delta}_{t}(x,\tilde{x})-\Delta(x,\tilde{x})\big|\leq\rho_{t}(x,\tilde{x}),\forall x,\tilde{x}\in\mathcal{X}\},

(I.4)

\displaystyle{\rm CI}:=\bigcap_{t}{\rm CI}_{t},

(I.5)

and the confidence radius

\displaystyle\rho_{t}(x,\tilde{x}):=2(\alpha_{t}+\xi_{t})+\sum_{j=1}^{N}\beta_{t,j}|\hat{\Delta}_{t,j}^{{\rm clip}_{2}}(x,\tilde{x})+\zeta_{t}(x,\tilde{x})|,

(I.6)

where

\displaystyle\alpha_{t}:=5\sqrt{\frac{d}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{v,T_{t}}}},\quad\beta_{t,j}:=\frac{5}{2}\sqrt{\frac{2\phi_{t,j}L_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}},

(I.7)

\displaystyle\xi_{t}:=25\sqrt{2}\frac{NL_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{m,T_{t}}},\quad\phi_{t,j}:=\min\left\{4\max\left\{\hat{p}_{t,j},\frac{25}{4}\frac{L_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}\right\},\frac{1}{4}\right\},

(I.8)

and
\zeta_{t}(x,\tilde{x})\in\mathbb{R}
can be any value. In particular, it can be the value that minimizes
\sum_{j=1}^{N}\beta_{t,j}|\hat{\Delta}_{t,j}(x,\tilde{x})+\zeta_{t}(x,\tilde{x})|
or it can be taken as
\varepsilon
. For simplicity, we will take
\zeta_{t}(x,\tilde{x})=\argmin_{\zeta_{t}(x,\tilde{x})\in\mathbb{R}}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\beta_{t,j}(\hat{\Delta}_{t,j}(x,\tilde{x})+\zeta_{t}(x,\tilde{x}))^{2}=-\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{N}\beta_{t,j}\hat{\Delta}_{t,j}(x,\tilde{x})}{\sum_{j=1}^{N}\beta_{t,j}}
for a simple and effective analytic expression in the algorithm.

I.1

Vector-Estimation Error

For the VE term
\Big|\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left(\hat{\Delta}_{t,j}(x,\tilde{x})-\Delta_{j}(x,\tilde{x})\right)\hat{p}_{t,j}\Big|
,
note that

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left(\hat{\Delta}_{t,j}(x,\tilde{x})-\Delta_{j}(x,\tilde{x})\right)\hat{p}_{t,j}\Big|\leq|x^{\top}(\hat{\Theta}_{t}-\Theta)\hat{p}_{t}|+|\tilde{x}^{\top}(\hat{\Theta}_{t}-\Theta)\hat{p}_{t}|

(I.9)

Lemma I.1
.

Given
x\in\mathcal{X}
and
T_{t}\geq\frac{d}{4}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{v,T_{t}}}
,

\mathbb{P}\left[|x^{\top}(\hat{\Theta}_{t}-\Theta)\hat{\mathbf{p}}_{t}|\geq\alpha_{t}\big|{\rm Good}\right]\leq\delta_{v,T_{t}}

(I.10)

Proof of Lemma
I.1
.

By the definition of the estimators in (
3.3
),

\displaystyle x^{\top}(\hat{\Theta}_{t}-\Theta)\hat{\mathbf{p}}_{t}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{N}x^{\top}(\hat{\theta}_{t,j}-\theta_{j}^{*})\hat{\mathbf{p}}_{t,j}

(I.11)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{N}x^{\top}\left(\frac{1}{T_{t,j}}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{T}_{t,j}}A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}x_{s}Y_{s,x_{s}}-\theta_{j}^{*}\right)\frac{T_{t,j}}{T_{t}}

(I.12)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T_{t}}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{T}_{t,j}}x^{\top}A(\lambda^{*})^{-1}x_{s}Y_{s,x_{s}}-x^{\top}\theta_{j}^{*}

(I.13)

By Lemma
E.1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[|x^{\top}(\hat{\Theta}_{t}-\Theta)\hat{\mathbf{p}}_{t}|\geq\alpha_{t}\big|\theta_{1:\infty},{\rm Good}\right]\leq\delta_{v,T_{t}}

(I.14)

By the property of conditional probability, we have the desired result.
∎

Therefore, conditional on
Good
, with probability at least
1-K\delta_{v,T_{t}}
,

{\rm VE}\leq 2\alpha_{t}
for any
x,\tilde{x}\in\mathcal{X}
.

I.2

Distribution-Estimation Error

Lemma I.2
.

Given
Good
and
T_{t}\geq\frac{2L_{\max}}{9}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}
, for any
j\in[N]
,

\mathbb{P}\left[|\hat{p}_{t,j}-p_{j}|\geq\beta_{t,j}\big|{\rm Good}\right]\leq\delta_{d,T_{t}}.

(I.15)

Additionally, with probability
1-N\delta_{d,T_{t}}
,

\Big|\sum_{j=1}^{N}\hat{\Delta}_{t,j}^{{\rm clip}_{2}}(x,\tilde{x})(\hat{p}_{t,j}-p_{j})\Big|\leq\sum_{j=1}^{N}\beta_{t,j}|\hat{\Delta}_{t,j}^{{\rm clip}_{2}}(x,\tilde{x})+\zeta_{t}(x,\tilde{x})|

(I.16)

where
\zeta_{t}(x,\tilde{x})
can be any value.

Proof of Lemma
I.2
.

Given any
j\in[N]
, and the stationary segment
l
, we denote
X_{j,l}:=\hat{L}_{l}\mathbbm{1}\{\theta_{j_{l}}^{*}=\theta_{j}^{*}\}-p_{j}\hat{L}_{l}
,
where
\hat{L}_{l}
is the total length of the Exp phases in the
l
th stationary segment. Note that
\mathbb{E}[X_{j,l}|\{\hat{L}_{l}\}_{l=1}^{l_{t}},{\rm Good}]=0,|X_{j,l}|\leq L_{\max}

a.s.
and
\var[X_{j,l}|\{\hat{L}_{l}\}_{l=1}^{l_{t}},{\rm Good}]=p_{j}(1-p_{j})\hat{L}_{l}^{2}\leq p_{j}(1-p_{j})\hat{L}_{l}L_{\max}
.
By Bernstein’s inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[|\sum_{l=1}^{l_{t}}X_{j,l}|\geq\epsilon\big|\{\hat{L}_{l}\}_{l=1}^{l_{t}},{\rm Good}\right]\leq 2\exp\left(-\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2\sum_{l=1}^{l_{t}}\var[X_{j,l}|{\rm Good}]+\frac{2}{3}L_{\max}\epsilon}\right)

(I.17)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\quad

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[|\frac{1}{T_{t}}\sum_{l=1}^{l_{t}}X_{j,l}|\geq\epsilon\big|\{\hat{L}_{l}\}_{l=1}^{l_{t}},{\rm Good}\right]\leq 2\exp\left(-\frac{T_{t}^{2}\epsilon^{2}}{2\sum_{l=1}^{l_{t}}\var[X_{j,l}|{\rm Good}]+\frac{2}{3}L_{\max}T_{t}\epsilon}\right)

(I.18)

As
T_{t,j}=\sum_{l=1}^{l_{t}}\hat{L}_{l}\mathbbm{1}\{\theta_{j_{l}}^{*}=\theta_{j}^{*}\},T_{t}=\sum_{l=1}^{l_{t}}\hat{L}_{l}
and
\sum_{l=1}^{l_{t}}\hat{L}_{l}=T_{t}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[|\hat{p}_{t,j}-p_{j}|\geq\epsilon\big|\{\hat{L}_{l}\}_{l=1}^{l_{t}},{\rm Good}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\left(-\frac{T_{t}^{2}\epsilon^{2}}{2\sum_{l=1}^{l_{t}}\var[X_{j,l}|\{\hat{L}_{l}\}_{l=1}^{l_{t}},{\rm Good}]+\frac{2}{3}L_{\max}T_{t}\epsilon}\right)

(I.19)

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\left(-\frac{T_{t}^{2}\epsilon^{2}}{2\sum_{l=1}^{l_{t}}p_{j}(1-p_{j})\hat{L}_{l}L_{\max}+\frac{2}{3}L_{\max}T_{t}\epsilon}\right)

(I.20)

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\left(-\frac{T_{t}\epsilon^{2}}{2p_{j}(1-p_{j})L_{\max}+\frac{2}{3}L_{\max}\epsilon}\right)

(I.21)

As the last bound is independent of
\{\hat{L}_{l}\}_{l=1}^{l_{t}}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[|\hat{p}_{t,j}-p_{j}|\geq\epsilon\big|{\rm Good}\right]\leq 2\exp\left(-\frac{T_{t}\epsilon^{2}}{2p_{j}(1-p_{j})L_{\max}+\frac{2}{3}L_{\max}\epsilon}\right)

(I.22)

If we want to upper bound the above by
\delta_{d,T_{t}}\in(0,1)
, i.e.,

2\exp\left(-\frac{T_{t}\epsilon^{2}}{2p_{j}(1-p_{j})L_{\max}+\frac{2}{3}L_{\max}\epsilon}\right)\leq\delta_{d,T_{t}}

(I.23)

we require

\epsilon\geq\frac{\frac{1}{3}L_{\max}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}+\sqrt{\left(\frac{1}{3}L_{\max}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}\right)^{2}+2T_{t}p_{j}(1-p_{j})L_{\max}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}}}{T_{t}}

(I.24)

In particular,

\epsilon=\tilde{\beta}_{t,j}:=\frac{5}{2}\max\left\{\frac{L_{\max}}{3T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}},\sqrt{\frac{2p_{j}(1-p_{j})L_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}}\right\}

(I.25)

satisfies the condition,
we have

\mathbb{P}\left[|\hat{p}_{t,j}-p_{j}|\geq\tilde{\beta}_{t,j}\big|{\rm Good}\right]\leq\delta_{d,T_{t}}.

(I.26)

A problem here is we do not have access to
p_{j}
during the dynamics, therefore, we adopt Lemma
I.2
to further upper bound
\tilde{\beta}_{t,j}
by
\beta_{t,j}
.

{restatable*}
lemmalemhbeta
Given any
j\in[N]
and
T_{t}\geq\frac{2L_{\max}}{9}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}
,
\tilde{\beta}_{t,j}\leq\beta_{t,j}
.

Therefore,

\mathbb{P}\left[|\hat{p}_{t,j}-p_{j}|\geq\beta_{t,j}\big|{\rm Good}\right]\leq\delta_{d,T_{t}}.

(I.27)

In addition, with probability at least
1-N\delta_{d,T_{t}}
,
we can upper bound DE term as:

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{j=1}^{N}\hat{\Delta}_{t,j}^{{\rm clip}_{2}}(x,\tilde{x})(\hat{p}_{t,j}-p_{j})\Big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left(\hat{\Delta}_{t,j}^{{\rm clip}_{2}}(x,\tilde{x})+\zeta_{t}(x,\tilde{x})\right)(\hat{p}_{t,j}-p_{j})\Big|

(I.28)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{N}\beta_{t,j}\big|\hat{\Delta}_{t,j}^{{\rm clip}_{2}}(x,\tilde{x})+\zeta_{t}(x,\tilde{x})\big|

(I.29)

where
\zeta_{t}(x,\tilde{x})\in\mathbb{R}
can be any value.

I.3

Residual-Estimation Error

RE is composed by the product of two deviations, i.e.,
\left(\Delta_{j}(x,\tilde{x})-\hat{\Delta}_{t,j}^{{\rm clip}_{2}}(x,\tilde{x})\right)
and
(\hat{p}_{t,j}-p_{j})
, as the time step
t
becomes large, we expect it will converge to zero fast. Thus, it is sufficient to have a coarse estimation of it.

Lemma I.3
.

For any
x\in\mathcal{X}
, conditional on that
|\hat{p}_{t,j}-p_{j}|\leq\beta_{t,j},\forall j\in[N]
,

\mathbb{P}\left[\Big|\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left(\Delta_{j}(x,\tilde{x})-\hat{\Delta}_{t,j}^{{\rm clip}_{2}}(x,\tilde{x})\right)(\hat{p}_{t,j}-p_{j})\Big|\geq\xi_{t}\Bigg|{\rm Good}\right]\leq N\delta_{m,T_{t}}

(I.30)

where
\xi_{t}:=25\sqrt{2}\frac{NL_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{m,T_{t}}}
.

Proof of Lemma
I.3
.

According to Lemma
E.1
, for any
x\in\mathcal{X},j\in[N]
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[|x^{\top}(\theta_{j}^{*}-\hat{\theta}_{t,j})|\geq 5\sqrt{\frac{d}{T_{t,j}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{m,T_{t}}}}\big|{\rm Good}\right]\leq\delta_{m,T_{t}}

(I.31)

if
T_{t,j}\geq\frac{d}{4}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{m,T_{t}}}
.
Note the fact that
\Delta_{j}(x,\tilde{x})\in[-2,2]
, hence,

\Big|\Delta_{j}(x,\tilde{x})-\hat{\Delta}_{t,j}^{{\rm clip}_{2}}(x,\tilde{x})\Big|\leq\Big|\Delta_{j}(x,\tilde{x})-\hat{\Delta}_{t,j}(x,\tilde{x})\Big|\leq|x^{\top}(\theta_{j}^{*}-\hat{\theta}_{t,j})|+|\tilde{x}^{\top}(\theta_{j}^{*}-\hat{\theta}_{t,j})|.

(I.32)

And
|x^{\top}(\theta_{j}^{*}-\hat{\theta}_{t,j})|\leq 3d
with probability
1
.
Denote

\psi_{t,j,1}:=\max\left\{\hat{p}_{t,j},\frac{d}{4T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{m,T_{t}}}\right\},\ \psi_{t,j,2}:=\max\left\{\hat{p}_{t,j},\frac{25}{4}\frac{L_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}\right\}.

(I.33)

We have
5
5

5

The last inequality can be loose. When each context has been visited at least once, the first summation in (
I.44
) can be ignored. For empirical performance, (
I.44
) should be used, while
\xi
is adopted for theoretical guarantees.

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left(\Delta_{j}(x,\tilde{x})-\hat{\Delta}_{t,j}^{{\rm clip}_{2}}(x,\tilde{x})\right)(\hat{p}_{t,j}-p_{j})\Big|

(I.34)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j:\psi_{t,j,1}>\hat{p}_{t,j}}\Big|\left(\Delta_{j}(x,\tilde{x})-\hat{\Delta}_{t,j}^{{\rm clip}_{2}}(x,\tilde{x})\right)\beta_{t,j}\Big|+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j:\psi_{t,j,1}=\hat{p}_{t,j},\\
\psi_{t,j,2}>\hat{p}_{t,j}\end{subarray}}\Big|\left(\Delta_{j}(x,\tilde{x})-\hat{\Delta}_{t,j,2}^{{\rm clip}_{2}}(x,\tilde{x})\right)\beta_{t,j}\Big|

(I.35)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j:\psi_{t,j,2}=\hat{p}_{t,j}}\Big|\left(\Delta_{j}(x,\tilde{x})-\hat{\Delta}_{t,j}^{{\rm clip}_{2}}(x,\tilde{x})\right)\beta_{t,j}\Big|

(I.36)

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}\sum_{j:\psi_{t,j,1}>\hat{p}_{t,j}}\Big|\left(\Delta_{j}(x,\tilde{x})-\hat{\Delta}_{t,j}^{{\rm clip}_{2}}(x,\tilde{x})\right)\beta_{t,j}\Big|+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j:\psi_{t,j,1}=\hat{p}_{t,j},\\
\psi_{t,j,2}>\hat{p}_{t,j}\end{subarray}}\Big|\left(\Delta_{j}(x,\tilde{x})-\hat{\Delta}_{t,j,2}^{{\rm clip}_{2}}(x,\tilde{x})\right)\beta_{t,j}\Big|

(I.37)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j:\psi_{t,j,2}=\hat{p}_{t,j}}|x^{\top}(\theta_{j}^{*}-\hat{\theta}_{t,j})\beta_{t,j}|+|\tilde{x}^{\top}(\theta_{j}^{*}-\hat{\theta}_{t,j})\beta_{t,j}|

(I.38)

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\leq}\sum_{j:\psi_{t,j,1}>\hat{p}_{t,j}}4\cdot\frac{5}{2}\cdot\frac{5\sqrt{2}}{2}\frac{L_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}

(I.39)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j:\psi_{t,j,1}=\hat{p}_{t,j},\\
\psi_{t,j,2}>\hat{p}_{t,j}\end{subarray}}\min\left\{4,2\cdot 5\sqrt{\frac{d}{T_{t,j}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{m,T_{t}}}}\right\}\cdot\frac{5}{2}\cdot\frac{5\sqrt{2}}{2}\frac{L_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}

(I.40)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j:\psi_{t,j,3}=\hat{p}_{t,j}}2\cdot 5\sqrt{\frac{d}{T_{t,j}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{m,T_{t}}}}\cdot\frac{5}{2}\sqrt{\frac{8\hat{p}_{t,j}L_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}}

(I.41)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j:\psi_{t,j,1}>\hat{p}_{t,j}}25\sqrt{2}\frac{L_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}

(I.42)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j:\psi_{t,j,1}=\hat{p}_{t,j},\\
\psi_{t,j,2}>\hat{p}_{t,j}\end{subarray}}\min\left\{4,10\sqrt{\frac{d}{T_{t,j}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{m,T_{t}}}}\right\}\cdot\frac{25\sqrt{2}}{4}\frac{L_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}

(I.43)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j:\psi_{t,j,2}=\hat{p}_{t,j}}50\sqrt{2}\frac{\sqrt{dL_{\max}}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{m,T_{t}}}

(I.44)

\displaystyle\leq 50\sqrt{2}\frac{NL_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{m,T_{t}}}=2\xi_{t}

(I.45)

where
(a)
adopts (
I.32
) and
(b)
is obtained by the following derivations:

(1)
\psi_{t,j,1}>\hat{p}_{t,j}
indicates
\hat{p}_{t,j}<\frac{d}{4T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{m,T_{t}}}<\frac{25}{4}\frac{L_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}
, and thus
\beta_{t,j}\leq\frac{5}{2}\cdot\frac{5\sqrt{2}}{2}\frac{L_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}
.In addition,
6
6

6

This demonstrates the usefulness of the clipping technique. Without the clipping technique, we can only get an upper bound of
6d
by utilizing (

) which introduces another

factor in
\xi_{t}
.
,

\Big|\Delta_{j}(x,\tilde{x})-\hat{\Delta}_{t,j}^{{\rm clip}_{2}}(x,\tilde{x})\Big|\leq 4.

(2)
\psi_{t,j,1}=\hat{p}_{t,j}
indicates
\hat{p}_{t,j}\leq\frac{d}{4T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{m,T_{t}}}
and
\psi_{t,j,2}>\hat{p}_{t,j}

, thus (

.

Therefore, conditional on Good, with probability at least

,
{\rm RE}\leq 2\xi_{t}

Proof of Lemma
G.5
.

By Lemma
I.1
,
I.2
and
I.3
, conditional on Good, with probability at least
1-(K\delta_{v,T_{t}}+N\delta_{d,T_{t}}+KN\delta_{m,T_{t}})
,

\big|\hat{\Delta}_{t}(x,\tilde{x})-\Delta(x,\tilde{x})\big|\leq\rho_{t}(x,\tilde{x})
.
This finishes the proof of the first result in Lemma
G.5
.

We then accumulates all the error probabilities.
By the choices of

\delta_{v,T_{t}}=\frac{\delta}{15KT_{t}^{3}},\delta_{d,T_{t}}=\frac{\delta}{15NT_{t}^{3}},\delta_{m,T_{t}}=\frac{\delta}{15KNT_{t}^{3}}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{T_{t}=1}^{\infty}K\delta_{v,T_{t}}+N\delta_{d,T_{t}}+KN\delta_{m,T_{t}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{T_{t}=1}^{\infty}K\cdot\frac{\delta}{15KT_{t}^{3}}+N\cdot\frac{\delta}{15NT_{t}^{3}}+KN\cdot\frac{\delta}{15KNT_{t}^{3}}

(I.46)

\displaystyle=\sum_{T_{t}=1}^{\infty}\frac{3\delta}{15T_{t}^{3}}

(I.47)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\delta}{4}

(I.48)

Note that there is an reversion step at Line
18
Algorithm
1
,
for each
T\in\mathbb{N}
, there are at most two time steps
t_{1}<t_{2}
with
T_{t_{1}}=T_{t_{2}}
. Therefore,

\sum_{t=1}^{\infty}K\delta_{v,T_{t}}+N\delta_{d,T_{t}}+KN\delta_{m,T_{t}}\leq 2\sum_{T_{t}=1}^{\infty}K\delta_{v,T_{t}}+N\delta_{d,T_{t}}+KN\delta_{m,T_{t}}\leq\frac{\delta}{2}

(I.49)

This proves the second statement.
∎

Appendix J

Upper Bound of
PS
\varepsilon
BAI
: Proof of Theorem
3.2

Theorem
3.2
is proved in this section by three steps.

•

Firstly, we show that the recommended arm
\hat{x}_{\varepsilon}
is an
\varepsilon
-best arm upon the termination of the algorithm in Lemma
J.1
.

•

Secondly, we present a sufficient condition for the termination of the algorithm in terms of
T_{t}
in Lemma
G.6
.

•

Lastly, we show that
T_{\tau}
is bounded by a constant fraction of
\tau
and thus, obtain an upper bound on
\tau
.

Step 1:

According to Lemma
G.5
,
{\rm CI}_{t}
holds with probability
1-(K\delta_{v,T_{t}}+N\delta_{d,T_{t}}+KN\delta_{m,T_{t}})
.
Conditional on the good event
Good
in (
G.4
) and the event
{\rm CI}_{t}
(the mean gaps are well approximated at time step
t
), we expect the recommended arm will be an
\varepsilon
-optimal arm.
This is formalized in the following lemma.

Lemma J.1
.

Conditional on
Good
and
{\rm CI}_{t}
, if the algorithm stops at time step
t
, the recommended arm
\hat{x}_{\varepsilon}=x_{t}^{*}
is an
\varepsilon
-best arm.

Proof of Lemma
J.1
.

If
\hat{x}_{\varepsilon}={x^{*}}
, the lemma holds.

If
\hat{x}_{\varepsilon}\neq{x^{*}}
,
according to the termination condition (
3.5
)

\min_{x:x\neq\hat{x}_{\varepsilon}}\hat{\Delta}_{t}(\hat{x}_{\varepsilon},x)-\rho_{t}(\hat{x}_{\varepsilon},x)>-\varepsilon

(J.1)

we have

\Delta(\hat{x}_{\varepsilon},{x^{*}})\geq\hat{\Delta}_{t}(\hat{x}_{\varepsilon},{x^{*}})-\rho_{t}(\hat{x}_{\varepsilon},{x^{*}})\geq\min_{x:x\neq\hat{x}_{\varepsilon}}\hat{\Delta}_{t}(\hat{x}_{\varepsilon},x)-\rho_{t}(\hat{x}_{\varepsilon},x)>-\varepsilon

(J.2)

where the first inequality holds due to
{\rm CI}_{t}
.
This indicates
\Delta({x^{*}},\hat{x}_{\varepsilon})\leq\varepsilon
and thus the recommended arm
\hat{x}_{\varepsilon}=x_{t}^{*}
is an
\varepsilon
-best arm.
∎

Step 2:

See
G.6

Proof of Lemma
G.6
.

By Lemma
J.1
,
\hat{x}_{\varepsilon}\in\mathcal{X}_{\varepsilon}
.
Note that for any
x\neq\hat{x}_{\varepsilon}
, when

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &2\rho_{t}(x,\hat{x}_{\varepsilon})\leq\Delta(x,\hat{x}_{\varepsilon})+\varepsilon,\quad\hat{x}_{\varepsilon}\neq x^{*}\mbox{ and }x=x^{*},\\
&\rho_{t}(\hat{x}_{\varepsilon},x)+\rho_{t}(x^{*},x)\leq\Delta(x^{*},x)+\varepsilon,\quad\mbox{otherwise.}\end{aligned}\right.

(J.3)

By utilizing
{\rm CI}_{t}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\hat{\Delta}_{t}(\hat{x}_{\varepsilon},x^{*})-\rho_{t}(\hat{x}_{\varepsilon},x^{*})\geq\hat{\Delta}_{t}(x^{*},\hat{x}_{\varepsilon})-\rho_{t}(\hat{x}_{\varepsilon},x^{*})\geq\Delta_{t}(x^{*},\hat{x}_{\varepsilon})-2\rho_{t}(x^{*},\hat{x}_{\varepsilon})\geq-\varepsilon,\\
&\hskip 260.0004pt\quad\hat{x}_{\varepsilon}\neq x^{*}\mbox{ and }x=x^{*},\\
&\hat{\Delta}_{t}(\hat{x}_{\varepsilon},x)-\rho_{t}(\hat{x}_{\varepsilon},x)\geq\hat{\Delta}_{t}(x^{*},x)-\rho_{t}(\hat{x}_{\varepsilon},x)\geq\Delta(x^{*},x)-\rho_{t}(x^{*},x)-\rho_{t}(\hat{x}_{\varepsilon},x)\geq-\varepsilon,\\
&\hskip 300.00046pt\quad\mbox{otherwise.}\end{aligned}\right.

(J.4)

Therefore, if (
J.3
) hold for all
x\neq\hat{x}_{\varepsilon}
, the algorithm must have stopped, i.e., it is sufficient to have the following for any
x\neq\hat{x}_{\varepsilon}
:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\rho_{t}(x^{*},\hat{x}_{\varepsilon})\leq\frac{\Delta(x^{*},\hat{x}_{\varepsilon})+\varepsilon}{2},\quad\hat{x}_{\varepsilon}\neq x^{*}\mbox{ and }x=x^{*},\\
&\rho_{t}(\hat{x}_{\varepsilon},x)\leq\frac{\Delta(x^{*},x)+\varepsilon}{2}\;,\rho_{t}(x^{*},x)\leq\frac{\Delta(x^{*},x)+\varepsilon}{2},\quad\mbox{otherwise.}\end{aligned}\right.

(J.5)

Lemma
J.2
gives an upper bound on the total number of time steps in Exp phases at which (
J.3
) must hold for any two arms
x_{\varepsilon}\in\mathcal{X}_{\varepsilon},x\in\mathcal{X}\setminus\{x_{\varepsilon}\}
.

Lemma J.2
.

For any two arms
(x_{\varepsilon},x)
, where
x\neq x_{\varepsilon},x^{*}
,
when

\displaystyle T_{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{6400\ln 6400\cdot d}{\left(\Delta(x^{*},x)+\varepsilon\right)^{2}}\ln\frac{Kd/\left(\Delta(x^{*},x)+\varepsilon\right)^{2}}{\delta}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+6400\ln 6400\cdot\mathrm{H}_{{\rm DE}}(x_{\varepsilon},x)\ln\frac{N\mathrm{H}_{{\rm DE}}(x_{\varepsilon},x)}{\delta}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{3200\sqrt{2}\ln 3200\sqrt{2}\cdot NL_{\max}}{\Delta(x^{*},x)+\varepsilon}\ln\frac{\frac{KNL_{\max}}{\Delta(x^{*},x)+\varepsilon}}{\delta}

(J.6)

\displaystyle=\tilde{O}\left(\frac{d}{\left(\Delta(x^{*},x)+\varepsilon\right)^{2}}\ln\frac{1}{\delta}+\mathrm{H}_{{\rm DE}}(x_{\varepsilon},x)\ln\frac{1}{\delta}+\frac{NL_{\max}}{\Delta(x^{*},x)+\varepsilon}\ln\frac{1}{\delta}\right)

(J.7)

where

\mathrm{H}_{{\rm DE}}(x_{\varepsilon},x):=\frac{L_{\max}}{\left(\Delta(x^{*},x)+\varepsilon\right)^{2}}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{N}\sqrt{\min\left\{16p_{j},\frac{1}{4}\right\}}|\Delta_{j}(x_{\varepsilon},x)+\varepsilon|\right)^{2},

we must have

\rho_{t}(x_{\varepsilon},x)\leq\frac{\Delta(x^{*},x)+\varepsilon}{2}

(J.8)

By taking the maximum of (
J.6
) over all
\varepsilon
-best arms and the suboptimal arms, we get

\displaystyle\tilde{T}

\displaystyle:=\max_{x_{\varepsilon}\in\mathcal{X}_{\varepsilon},x\neq x_{\varepsilon},x^{*}}\frac{6400\ln 6400\cdot d}{\left(\Delta(x^{*},x)+\varepsilon\right)^{2}}\ln\frac{Kd/\left(\Delta(x^{*},x)+\varepsilon\right)^{2}}{\delta}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+6400\ln 6400\cdot\mathrm{H}_{{\rm DE}}(x_{\varepsilon},x)\ln\frac{N\mathrm{H}_{{\rm DE}}(x_{\varepsilon},x)}{\delta}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{3200\sqrt{2}\ln 3200\sqrt{2}\cdot NL_{\max}}{\Delta(x^{*},x)+\varepsilon}\ln\frac{\frac{KNL_{\max}}{\Delta(x^{*},x)+\varepsilon}}{\delta}

(J.9)

\displaystyle=\tilde{O}\left(\max_{x_{\varepsilon}\in\mathcal{X}_{\varepsilon},x\neq x_{\varepsilon},x^{*}}\frac{d}{\left(\Delta(x^{*},x)+\varepsilon\right)^{2}}\ln\frac{1}{\delta}+\mathrm{H}_{{\rm DE}}(x_{\varepsilon},x)\ln\frac{1}{\delta}+\frac{NL_{\max}}{\Delta(x^{*},x)+\varepsilon}\ln\frac{1}{\delta}\right).

(J.10)

∎

Proof of Lemma
J.2
.

Let
c_{v},c_{d},c_{m}\in(0,1)
be constants, satisfying
c_{v}+c_{d}+2c_{m}=1
. By the definition of
\rho_{t}(x_{\varepsilon},x)
in (
3.4
), it is sufficient to have

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &2\alpha_{t}\leq c_{v}\frac{\Delta(x^{*},x)+\varepsilon}{2}\\
&\sum_{j=1}^{N}\beta_{t,j}|\hat{\Delta}_{t,j}^{{\rm clip}_{2}}(x_{\varepsilon},x)+\zeta_{t}(x_{\varepsilon},x)|\leq(c_{d}+c_{m})\frac{\Delta(x^{*},x)+\varepsilon}{2}\\
&2\xi_{t}\leq c_{m}\frac{\Delta(x^{*},x)+\varepsilon}{2}\end{aligned}\right.

(J.11)

where we take
\zeta_{t}(x_{\varepsilon},x)=\varepsilon
for theoretical simplicity, but we adopt
\zeta_{t}(x,\tilde{x})=\argmin_{\zeta_{t}(x,\tilde{x})\in\mathbb{R}}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\beta_{t,j}(\hat{\Delta}_{t,j}(x,\tilde{x})+\zeta_{t}(x,\tilde{x}))^{2}=-\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{N}\beta_{t,j}\hat{\Delta}_{t,j}(x,\tilde{x})}{\sum_{j=1}^{N}\beta_{t,j}}
in the algorithm, which still enjoys the theoretical guarantee.

VE Term

According to the definition of
\alpha_{t}
, we need to bound:

\displaystyle\alpha_{t}\leq c_{v}\frac{\Delta(x^{*},x)+\varepsilon}{4}

(J.12)

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\quad

\displaystyle 5\sqrt{\frac{d}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{v,T_{t}}}}\leq c_{v}\frac{\Delta(x^{*},x)+\varepsilon}{4}

(J.13)

By a coarse estimation, it is sufficient to have

\displaystyle T_{t}\geq\frac{\bar{c}_{v}d}{\left(\Delta(x^{*},x)+\varepsilon\right)^{2}}\ln\frac{Kd/\left(\Delta(x^{*},x)+\varepsilon\right)^{2}}{\delta}=O\left(\frac{d}{\left(\Delta(x^{*},x)+\varepsilon\right)^{2}}\ln\frac{Kd/\left(\Delta(x^{*},x)+\varepsilon\right)^{2}}{\delta}\right)

(J.14)

where
\bar{c}_{v}=\frac{6400}{c_{v}^{2}}\ln\frac{6400}{c_{v}^{2}}
.

DE Term

The difficulty lies at the
DE
term.

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{N}\beta_{t,j}|\hat{\Delta}_{t,j}^{{\rm clip}_{2}}(x_{\varepsilon},x)+\zeta_{t}(x_{\varepsilon},x)|

(J.15)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{N}\beta_{t,j}|\hat{\Delta}_{t,j}^{{\rm clip}_{2}}(x_{\varepsilon},x)+\varepsilon|

(J.16)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j:\psi_{t,j}>\hat{p}_{t,j}}\beta_{t,j}|\hat{\Delta}_{t,j}^{{\rm clip}_{2}}(x_{\varepsilon},x)+\varepsilon|

(J.17)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j:\psi_{t,j}=\hat{p}_{t,j}}\beta_{t,j}|\Delta_{j}(x_{\varepsilon},x)+\varepsilon|+\beta_{t,j}|\hat{\Delta}_{t,j}^{{\rm clip}_{2}}(x_{\varepsilon},x)-\Delta_{j}(x_{\varepsilon},x)|

(J.18)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j:\psi_{t,j}>\hat{p}_{t,j}}\beta_{t,j}(2+\varepsilon)+\sum_{j:\psi_{t,j}=\hat{p}_{t,j}}\beta_{t,j}|\hat{\Delta}_{t,j}^{{\rm clip}_{2}}(x_{\varepsilon},x)-\Delta_{j}(x_{\varepsilon},x)|

(J.19)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j:\psi_{t,j}=\hat{p}_{t,j}}\beta_{t,j}|\Delta_{j}(x_{\varepsilon},x)+\varepsilon|

(J.20)

\displaystyle\leq 2\xi_{t}+\sum_{j:\psi_{t,j}=\hat{p}_{t,j}}\beta_{t,j}|\Delta_{j}(x_{\varepsilon},x)+\varepsilon|

(J.21)

We will upper bound
2\xi_{t}
by
c_{m}\frac{\Delta(x^{*},x)+\varepsilon}{2}
first;
the second term will be included in the RE term and analyzed later.
Therefore, it is sufficient to have

\sum_{j:\psi_{t,j}=\hat{p}_{t,j}}\beta_{t,j}|\Delta_{j}(x_{\varepsilon},x)+\varepsilon|\leq c_{d}\frac{\Delta(x^{*},x)+\varepsilon}{2}

(J.22)

By the definition of
\beta_{t,j}
in (
I.6
) and Lemma
J
, we get

\beta_{t,j}=\frac{5}{2}\sqrt{\frac{2\phi_{t,j}L_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}}\leq\frac{5}{2}\sqrt{\frac{2\min\left\{16p_{j},\frac{1}{4}\right\}L_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}}

(J.23)

{restatable*}

lemmalemupbdphi
If
\psi_{t,j}=\hat{p}_{t,j}
,
then

\phi_{t,j}\leq\min\left\{16p_{j},\frac{1}{4}\right\}.

(J.24)

Therefore, in order for (
J.22
) to hold, it is sufficient to have

\displaystyle\sum_{j:\psi_{t,j,2}=\hat{p}_{t,j}}\frac{5}{2}\sqrt{\frac{2\min\left\{16p_{j},\frac{1}{4}\right\}L_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}}|\Delta_{j}(x_{\varepsilon},x)+\varepsilon|\leq c_{d}\frac{\Delta(x^{*},x)+\varepsilon}{2}

(J.25)

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\quad

\displaystyle\frac{5}{2}\sqrt{\frac{2L_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}}\sum_{j:\psi_{t,j,2}=\hat{p}_{t,j}}\sqrt{\min\left\{16p_{j},\frac{1}{4}\right\}}|\Delta_{j}(x_{\varepsilon},x)+\varepsilon|\leq c_{d}\frac{\Delta(x^{*},x)+\varepsilon}{2}

(J.26)

\displaystyle\Leftarrow\quad

\displaystyle\frac{5}{2}\sqrt{\frac{2L_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\sqrt{\min\left\{16p_{j},\frac{1}{4}\right\}}|\Delta_{j}(x_{\varepsilon},x)+\varepsilon|\leq c_{d}\frac{\Delta(x^{*},x)+\varepsilon}{2}

(J.27)

\displaystyle\Leftarrow\quad

\displaystyle T_{t}\geq\bar{c}_{d}\mathrm{H}_{{\rm DE}}(x_{\varepsilon},x)\ln\frac{N\mathrm{H}_{{\rm DE}}(x_{\varepsilon},x)}{\delta}=O\left(\mathrm{H}_{{\rm DE}}(x_{\varepsilon},x)\ln\frac{N\mathrm{H}_{{\rm DE}}(x_{\varepsilon},x)}{\delta}\right)

(J.28)

where
\bar{c}_{d}=\frac{100}{c_{d}^{2}}\ln\frac{100}{c_{d}^{2}}
.

RE Term
By the definition of
\xi_{t}
in (
I.6
), it is sufficient to have

\displaystyle 25\sqrt{2}\frac{NL_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{m,T_{t}}}\leq c_{m}\frac{\Delta(x^{*},x)+\varepsilon}{2}

\displaystyle\Leftarrow\quad

.

By taking

,

\displaystyle T_{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{6400\ln 6400\cdot d}{\left(\Delta(x^{*},x)+\varepsilon\right)^{2}}\ln\frac{Kd/\left(\Delta(x^{*},x)+\varepsilon\right)^{2}}{\delta}+6400\ln 6400\cdot\mathrm{H}_{{\rm DE}}(x_{\varepsilon},x)\ln\frac{N\mathrm{H}_{{\rm DE}}(x_{\varepsilon},x)}{\delta}

(J.31)

(J.32)

(J.33)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\left.+\mathrm{H}_{{\rm DE}}(x_{\varepsilon},x)\ln\frac{N\mathrm{H}_{{\rm DE}}(x_{\varepsilon},x)}{\delta}+\frac{NL_{\max}}{\Delta(x^{*},x)+\varepsilon}\ln\frac{\frac{KNL_{\max}}{\Delta(x^{*},x)+\varepsilon}}{\delta}\right)

(J.34)

\displaystyle=\tilde{O}\left(\frac{d}{\left(\Delta(x^{*},x)+\varepsilon\right)^{2}}\ln\frac{1}{\delta}+\mathrm{H}_{{\rm DE}}(x_{\varepsilon},x)\ln\frac{1}{\delta}+\frac{NL_{\max}}{\Delta(x^{*},x)+\varepsilon}\ln\frac{1}{\delta}\right).

(J.35)

Step 3:

See
3.2

Proof of Theorem
3.2
.

By
Assumption
1
,
and the choice of the parameters, Lemma
G.4
indicates Good is guaranteed with probability at least
1-\frac{\delta}{2}
.

Note that some arm pulls are not counted in
T_{t}
:

•

In every
\gamma
time steps, Algorithm
1
enters the CD phase to select a CD sample.

•

When a changepoint is detected, Algorithm
1
steps into the CA phase, where
\frac{w}{2}
arms are sampled in Algorithm
4
.

•

During the CA phase, we abandon
\frac{w\gamma}{2}
samples.

Therefore, when
t
is large (or after the first stationary segment)

\displaystyle t\leq T_{t}\cdot\frac{\gamma}{\gamma-1}\frac{L_{\min}}{L_{\min}-w\gamma-\frac{w}{2}}\leq T_{t}\cdot\frac{\gamma}{\gamma-1}\frac{L_{\min}}{L_{\min}-\frac{3w\gamma}{2}}.

(J.36)

As
\gamma\geq 2
and
w\gamma
are set to be upper bounded by
\frac{L_{\min}}{3}
, so the fractions above is upper bounded by an absolute constant
4
, e.g., with
\gamma=2
and
w=\frac{L_{\min}}{6}
, the constant is
4
.
Therefore,
t
is of the same order as
T_{t}
. By Lemma
G.6
, (
3.8
) holds with probability
1-\delta
.
∎

Appendix K

Analysis of
PS
\varepsilon
BAI
: Technical Lemmas

\lemhbeta

Proof of Lemma
I.2
.

Recall

\displaystyle\phi_{t,j}=\min\left\{4\max\left\{\hat{p}_{t,j},\frac{25}{4}\frac{L_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}\right\},\frac{1}{4}\right\}

(K.1)

The proof is scheduled in
2
steps.

Step 1
:
Upper bound
\sqrt{\frac{2p_{j}(1-p_{j})L_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}}
.

When
\frac{L_{\max}}{3T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{2p_{j}(1-p_{j})L_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}}
,
we have

\tilde{\beta}_{t,j}=\frac{5}{2}\sqrt{\frac{2p_{j}(1-p_{j})L_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}}

(K.2)

As
p_{j}(1-p_{j})\leq\frac{1}{4},\forall p_{j}\in(0,1)
, this naiv̈e bound gives

\tilde{\beta}_{t,j}\leq\frac{5}{2}\sqrt{\frac{2\cdot\frac{1}{4}\cdot L_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}}
.

If
T_{t,j}>0
, according to Lemma
K.1
,

\displaystyle\tilde{\beta}_{t,j}

\displaystyle=\frac{5}{2}\sqrt{\frac{2p_{j}(1-p_{j})L_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}}

(K.3)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{5}{2}\sqrt{\frac{2p_{j}T_{t,j}L_{\max}}{T_{t}T_{t,j}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}}

(K.4)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{5}{2}\sqrt{\frac{2\hat{p}_{t,j}L_{\max}}{T_{t}}\cdot 4\max\left\{1,\frac{25}{4}\frac{L_{\max}}{T_{t,j}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}\right\}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}},

(K.5)

thus we have

\tilde{\beta}_{t,j}\leq\frac{5}{2}\sqrt{\frac{2\phi_{t,j}L_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}}

(K.6)

If
T_{t,j}=0
, i.e., the latent context
j
has never been observed and
\hat{p}_{t,j}=0
, we have

\displaystyle\tilde{\beta}_{t,j}

\displaystyle=\frac{5}{2}\sqrt{\frac{2p_{j}(1-p_{j})L_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}}

(K.7)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{5}{2}\sqrt{\frac{2\tilde{\beta}_{t,j}(1-\tilde{\beta}_{t,j})L_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}}

(K.8)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\quad

\displaystyle\tilde{\beta}_{j}(t,\delta)\leq\frac{\frac{25}{8}L_{\max}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}}{T_{t}+\frac{25}{8}L_{\max}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}}\leq\frac{25}{8}\frac{L_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}

(K.9)

Take the trivial bound into consideration and observe that
\phi_{t,j}=\min\{\frac{25L_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}},\frac{1}{4}\}
and that

\frac{25}{8}\frac{L_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}\leq\frac{25\sqrt{2}L_{\max}}{2T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}=\frac{5}{2}\sqrt{\frac{2\phi_{t,j}L_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}}.

Thus, (
K.6
) also holds for
T_{t,j}=0
.

To conclude
, we have

\displaystyle\tilde{\beta}_{t,j}

\displaystyle=\frac{5}{2}\max\left\{\frac{L_{\max}}{3T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}},\sqrt{\frac{2p_{j}(1-p_{j})L_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{5}{2}\max\left\{\frac{L_{\max}}{3T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}},\sqrt{\frac{2\phi_{t,j}L_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}}\right\}

(K.10)

Step 2
: Simplify the bound. Recall that

\phi_{t,j}=\min\left\{4\max\left\{\hat{p}_{t,j},\frac{25}{4}\frac{L_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}\right\},\frac{1}{4}\right\}

As
T_{t}\geq\frac{2L_{\max}}{9}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}
, we have

\frac{L_{\max}}{3T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{2\cdot\frac{1}{4}L_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}}

(K.11)

According to the definition of
\phi
,

if
\hat{p}_{t,j}\geq\frac{25}{4}\frac{L_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}
, we have

\frac{L_{\max}}{3T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}\leq\frac{5\sqrt{2}L_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{2\cdot 4\hat{p}_{t,j}L_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}}

(K.12)

If
\hat{p}_{t,j}\leq\frac{25}{4}\frac{L_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}
, we have

\frac{L_{\max}}{3T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}\leq\frac{25\sqrt{2}L_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}=\sqrt{\frac{2L_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}\cdot 25\frac{L_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}}

(K.13)

Thus

\max\left\{\frac{L_{\max}}{3T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}},\sqrt{\frac{2\phi_{t,j}L_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}}\right\}=\sqrt{\frac{2\phi_{t,j}L_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}}

(K.14)

This gives us the desired result.
∎

Lemma K.1
.

When
\tilde{\beta}_{t,j}=\frac{5}{4}\sqrt{\frac{2p_{j}(1-p_{j})L_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}}
and
T_{t,j}>0
,
we have

\frac{p_{j}}{T_{t,j}}\leq\frac{4}{T_{t}}\max\left\{1,\frac{25}{16}\frac{L_{\max}}{T_{t,j}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}\right\}

(K.15)

Proof of Lemma
K.1
.

\displaystyle\frac{p_{j}}{T_{t,j}}

\displaystyle=\frac{p_{j}T_{t}}{T_{t,j}T_{t}}

(K.16)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\hat{p}_{t,j}T_{t}+\frac{5}{4}\sqrt{2p_{j}(1-p_{j})L_{\max}T_{t}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}}}{T_{t,j}T_{t}}

(K.17)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T_{t}}+\frac{5}{4}\sqrt{\frac{p_{j}}{T_{t,j}}}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{1}{T_{t,j}}-\frac{p_{j}}{T_{t,j}}}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{2L_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}}

(K.18)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\quad

\displaystyle\frac{p_{j}}{T_{t,j}}\leq\frac{4}{T_{t}}\max\left\{1,\frac{25}{16}\frac{L_{\max}}{T_{t,j}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}\right\}

(K.19)

∎

\lemupbdphi

Proof of Lemma
J
.

Recall to the definition of
\phi_{t,j}
in (
I.6
) and
\psi_{t,j}
in (
I.33
),

\phi_{t,j}=\min\left\{4\max\left\{\hat{p}_{t,j},\frac{25}{4}\frac{L_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}\right\},\frac{1}{4}\right\},\ \psi_{t,j}=\max\left\{\hat{p}_{t,j},\frac{25}{4}\frac{L_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}\right\},

(K.20)

we have

\hat{p}_{t,j}\geq\frac{25}{4}\frac{L_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}},\quad\phi_{t,j}=\min\left\{4\hat{p}_{t,j},\frac{1}{4}\right\}

(K.21)

We only need to upper bound
\hat{p}_{t,j}
.

By (
I.26
),

\displaystyle\psi_{t,j}=\hat{p}_{t,j}\leq p_{j}+\frac{5}{2}\max\left\{\frac{L_{\max}}{3T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}},\sqrt{\frac{2p_{j}(1-p_{j})L_{\max}}{T_{t}}\ln\frac{2}{\delta_{d,T_{t}}}}\right\}

(K.22)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\quad

\displaystyle\psi_{t,j}\leq p_{j}+\frac{5}{2}\max\left\{\frac{4}{75}\psi_{t,j},\sqrt{p_{j}(1-p_{j})\frac{8}{25}\psi_{t,j}}\right\}

(K.23)

\displaystyle\hphantom{\psi_{t,j} }=p_{j}+\max\left\{\frac{4}{15}\psi_{t,j},\sqrt{2p_{j}(1-p_{j})\psi_{t,j}}\right\}

(K.24)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\quad

\displaystyle\psi_{t,j}\leq 4p_{j}

(K.25)

Thus
\hat{p}_{t,j}=\psi_{t,j}\leq 4p_{j}
, and
\phi_{t,j}\leq\min\left\{16p_{j},\frac{1}{4}\right\}
.
∎

Appendix L

Upper Bound of
PS
\varepsilon
BAI
+
: Proof of Theorem
3.3

See
3.3

Proof.

We let
\tau_{1}
denote the stopping time of
PS
\varepsilon
BAI
,
\tau_{2}
denote the stopping time of
N
\varepsilon
BAI
, and
\tau
denote the stopping time of
PS
\varepsilon
BAI
+
when an identical sequence of samples and returns are applied to them. By the design of these algorithms, we have

\displaystyle\tau\leq\min\{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}\}.

(L.1)

Part I
: Prove that
\mathbb{P}(\mathring{x}_{\varepsilon}\notin\mathcal{X}_{\varepsilon})\leq\delta
.

Let event
\mathcal{E}_{1}=\mathsf{1}\{\tau_{1}<\tau_{2}\}\cap\mathsf{1}\{\text{{\sc PS$\varepsilon$BAI}\ returns arm ${x}_{\varepsilon}$ when it terminates}\}
. Recall that
\dot{x}_{\varepsilon}
denotes the recommended arm of
N
\varepsilon
BAI
when it terminates and
\mathring{x}_{\varepsilon}
denotes the recommended arm of
PS
\varepsilon
BAI
+
when it terminates. The design of algorithms indicates that:

\mathring{x}_{\varepsilon}={x}_{\varepsilon}
when
\mathcal{E}_{1}
occurs, and
\mathring{x}_{\varepsilon}=\dot{x}_{\varepsilon}
when
\mathcal{E}_{1}^{c}
occurs.

(L.2)

Moreover, with the performance guarantees of
N
\varepsilon
BAI
and
PS
\varepsilon
BAI
(shown in Proposition
3.1
and Theorem
3.2
